The console's generated types follow.
- `npx @parcha/agentrun` with `AGENTRUN_PUBLIC_URL`, on a server whose owner has no password yet, now shows the server's "Set up this server: <public URL>/setup#code=…" line on stderr. Setup at a public address asks for that code, and the command hid the line, so a fresh server behind HTTPS couldn't be set up from its own address.
- When the server stops before printing anything on stdout (its reason on stderr only, such as another server holding the data root), `npx @parcha/agentrun` now says "AgentRun did not start; the server's own message, if it gave one, is above." where it said the server printed nothing.
- The Render blueprint deploys a 2 GB instance (`1c-2g`) with a 10 GB disk, about $27.50 a month. On a Starter's 512 MB and 1 GB disk, Claude Code's first-use install filled the disk; health turned 503 and Render restarted the service.
- `render.yaml` generates `AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY` as it generates `AGENTRUN_TOKEN`. A service made from the blueprint can now save model and tool keys, MCP server credentials and webhook trigger secrets from its first start, with no key added by hand. A service that already has `AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY` keeps its own value.
- Packages take TypeBox's `Type`, `Static` and `TSchema` from typebox, never through pi-ai, pi-agent-core, pi-durable or chord, which drop those re-exports from pi 1.5. serve, pi-browser and pi-agent-learn now depend on typebox 1.3.27 directly (the version pi-ai 1.1 resolves); harness and agentrun-pi keep it as a host-provided peer. Tool schemas serialize exactly as before, so tool arguments are checked the same. harness's `shownShots` has a named return type, `ShownShots`.
- A workflow's model node submits on the runner. Every model request on the runner goes through the session's model proxy, whose policy drops each tool definition it is not given, and a workflow's proxy was given none: a node's request reached its model with no `submit`, so no workflow could finish there. The workflow's proxy now admits the node's whole offered set by name, its `submit` and the tools its workflow's model nodes name, on Anthropic's Messages and OpenAI's Responses alike, as a pi session's proxy admits its own tools. agentrun-pi exports the tool's name as `SUBMIT_TOOL`.
- `node scripts/changes.mjs --check` no longer reads a package that catches up to the workspace version as a release commit: a package whose manifest moves to the version the root `package.json` already holds at the merge base (one added behind the others, as `@parcha/agentrun-computer` was at 0.1.0-beta.30) is an ordinary pull request, and a fragment beside it passes. A release moves the root version too, so its bumps still count, and an unfolded fragment on one is still refused.
- `actions/setup-go` is pinned by commit (v5) in the release's publish job, which holds the npm OIDC token, and in CI's build and computer-docker jobs. The `@parcha/agentrun-computer` README states what every install carries (both daemons and the conformance code) and the archive's size.
- The held package `@parcha/agentrun-learn` (`packages/learn`) is removed. Nothing imported it, and it was never published to npm. The server's learning code lives in `@parcha/agentrun-serve` (`src/learn/`) and is unchanged. The release lists, `verify-packages`, the source export, the build and test orders and the documentation no longer name it, and the release preflight's held-package tests use `@parcha/agentrun-computer`, which is still held.
- Imports follow the layer direction. `npm run check:direction` (`scripts/check-direction.mjs`, run in CI's checks shard) gives each workspace package one layer: protocol, kernel, plug-in, subsystem, server or surface. A protocol reaches nothing, the kernel reaches protocols, a plug-in reaches protocols and the kernel, a subsystem also reaches other subsystems, the server reaches anything and a surface reaches no workspace package. An edge is a manifest dependency, an import, export, `import()`, `require` or `import.meta.resolve` of a workspace package, a relative path into another package, or a surface naming one of the server's data files. Today's 23 wrong edges are listed as exceptions, each with its count, reason and fix. A new wrong edge fails, and so does an exception that no longer occurs, so the list shrinks as the fixes land.
- A test covers `npx @parcha/agentrun` whose reader closes its pipes once it has the address (`| head -n 1`). A box image line said after that goes nowhere, and the command and its server keep running until they are stopped.
- serve's daytona-boxes test that run A's token reaches nothing of run B's box no longer fails when run B makes a call of its own at the same moment (a late call at its turn's end). Run A's calls now name a file only they name, and the test looks for that name in what reached run B's box. Run A's own box, asked the same way, shows the name is seen when a call does get through.
- serve's test cleanup no longer fails when part of a scratch tree is removed while it walks it. A fake box's command, or a server still closing, removes its own spool directories, and the walk that makes the tree removable now passes over a directory that is already gone. That fixes daytona-boxes' "a run found waiting at a start is pulled then" failing in its after hook with ENOENT on a box's `tmp/pi-transport/<id>/in`.
- `npm run test:affected` (`node scripts/test-affected.mjs`) runs only the test files a change reaches: a changed test file, the tests that import a changed source file directly or transitively (imports read from the parse tree, `dist/` mapped back to `src/`, workspace names resolved through `exports`), a package's every test when its `package.json`, tsconfig or build script changes, and the tests that name a changed file where an import can't say. A shared root (root manifests, `scripts/`, `.github/`) adds only its importers, and a fast smoke file per package always runs. It prints a command per suite, or the files (`--list`), or why each file was picked (`--json`). CI still runs every suite. pi-browser's test runner (`node scripts/run-tests.mjs`) now runs only the test files it is given.
- Tests only: the workspace runner's Ctrl-C test no longer loses a workspace's SIGTERM line. Its fixture step installed its SIGTERM handler after announcing its pid, so a stop landing in between ended the step unlogged.
- Tests that start a server with an environment of their own (the server-record test, the npx command's `cli` and `follow` tests) set `AGENTRUN_BOXES=none` instead of dropping it, and so do the decision demos' runner and the smokes' faux server unless the caller names boxes. Since the Docker box is on by default, these made real Docker calls on CI runners, built the box image and raced their own cleanup.
- Tests no longer remove a temporary directory while a server or child they started in it still writes there. node:test runs a test's after-hooks in the order they were added, so a removal added as the directory was made ran before the stop added after it (ENOTEMPTY on CI). The serve suite's `scratch()` and the npx command's tests now remove their directories once the file's tests are done, and the helpers that make and stop their own processes add the stop before the removal.
- Groundwork for a canary from main, published under npm's `canary` dist-tag. No workflow publishes one yet.
  `scripts/canary.mjs versions <run> <sha>` sets every listed package, held ones too, to its canary version, and every exact pin between them (dependencies, peers, optional and dev). `x.y.z` becomes `x.y.(z+1)-canary.<run>.g<sha7>` and `x.y.z-beta.N` becomes `x.y.z-canary.<run>.g<sha7>`. It refuses a tree that is already a canary.
  `scripts/canary.mjs plan <sha>` writes `.release/release-plan.json` from the archives `verify:packages` verified. It checks the bytes, the commit the versions name, and that the verification passed.
  `scripts/verify-published.mjs` takes `--canary`. With it, every package of the plan must be `<x.y.z>-canary.<run>.g<sha>`, all on one suffix, and a release plan is refused. Without it, nothing changes and a canary plan is refused. An unknown or repeated flag stops at the usage line before any registry read.
- The docs match the beta's code. The server README describes an agent's computer (`computer: {kind, provider}`, Settings → Computers, Mode 1 and Mode 2 on Archil) in place of the boxes, says where `bash` runs (in a computer that mounts the run's folder; on Archil's serverless exec in Mode 2, with its 295 s bound, no stop once sent, no kept file mode and no link followed; nowhere in Mode 1 without such a computer), that an escalated workflow run ends `completed` with a `review`, the run's `pending` kinds and the webhook's, that a trigger's run is titled from its trigger, that a GitHub App token is minted again 5 minutes before it expires, that an agent keeps its own tool pins (the template READMEs too), and that a server in a container can't start a Docker sandbox yet. The contributing guides build the computer daemon before `verify:packages`.
- The root README is rewritten for a first-time reader: what AgentRun does, a three-step start (`npx @parcha/agentrun` or Deploy to Render), a CLI example that makes an agent, starts a capped run, answers its question and reads its file, three diagrams (the server, store, runners and hands; the computer kinds; the learning loop), short sections on templates, computers, workflows, learning, security and deploying, and a short list of what comes next. The package inventory moved out: each package's own README is its reference. The support example and the research demo moved to `docs/examples.md`, and the docs that linked to them in the README link there.

## 0.1.0-beta.31 (2026-11-06)

- `npx @parcha/agentrun` manages agents and runs from the terminal: `agents list|get|create|update`, `runs start|get|list|message|stop|cancel|raise-cap`, `triggers ...`, `usage`, `stats` and a command for every other operation the server's OpenAPI document offers, plus `api METHOD PATH [--header 'NAME: VALUE']` for any route. An operation the server turns off has no command, and neither has `learning approve`, which takes the owner's password session in the console and refuses every token. `npx @parcha/agentrun help` lists the commands and `<command> --help` gives a command's arguments. Path parameters are positional, each encoded whole (`learning read AGENT skills/find-policies/SKILL.md`) except a file's path, which keeps its slashes (`home read AGENT notes/a.md`); query parameters, header parameters (`--idempotency-key`, `--if-match`) and a JSON body's top-level scalar fields are flags (`--max-cost-usd 2`); `--body JSON|@FILE|-` gives the whole body, its fields under the flags, and `--body-type TYPE` sends a body of another type the route takes as it is (`files create --path notes.txt --body @notes.txt --body-type text/plain`). A JSON body with no flags goes byte for byte. The response's JSON goes to stdout (indented on a terminal), a file's bytes exactly as the server sent them, and an error's JSON to stderr; exit codes are 0 ok, 1 the server refused, 2 bad usage, 3 the server cannot be reached, 4 no token or the token refused. A command whose body carries a secret (`tool-keys save`, `mcp-servers save|preview`, and `api` on any path that leads to one) takes its body only from stdin or a file.
  The server and token come from `--base-url`, else `AGENTRUN_BASE_URL` and `AGENTRUN_API_KEY`, else `base_url` and `api_key` in `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/agentrun/config.json`, else the server started on this machine with the same `--data` (default `~/.agentrun`). The token is never a flag and never printed, a key that is not a valid header value is refused before any request, and the local server's token goes to that server only. The local token is read from `DIR/.token` alone, and the config file, `DIR/.server.json` and `DIR/.token` are used only when they are regular files of the user's own with no group or other permission (exit 4 otherwise). `api` refuses a PATH with a `.` or `..` segment, plain or encoded, or an empty one. A reader that closes stdout early (`| head`) ends the command quietly. With no positional argument the command still starts the server, and `import` and `reset-password` are still the server's.
  `agentrun-serve` writes `DIR/.server.json` (`{base_url, pid, token_file}`, mode 0600, never the token; `token_file` is absent when the token came from `AGENTRUN_TOKEN`) once it listens and removes it on a clean exit; a file whose server is gone is reported as such, exit 3. The command table is generated from the server's document by `node scripts/generate-clients.mjs`, with names in `scripts/clients/resources.json`; its header hashes only what the table is made of, and `--check` fails when the committed table differs.
- The Docker box is on by default. A server started with `AGENTRUN_BOXES` not set at all (a plain `npx @parcha/agentrun`) probes Docker at its start, as if `docker` were listed, and offers it when Docker answers, so a new agent lands in the Docker box with a shell. Where Docker does not answer, new agents run on the runner, and the banner (`Boxes: none, so new agents run on the runner. Docker isn't running on this machine (no docker command on PATH): install or start Docker, then restart AgentRun.`), `capabilities.places.default_reason`, docker's `reason` and the console's new-agent form say why. With Docker on, the banner says `Boxes: docker (on by default; set AGENTRUN_BOXES to change).`
  An `AGENTRUN_BOXES` you set still wins: a list probes only what it names, and an empty value or `none` sets up no box (`none` stands alone). Daytona, the VM box and bwrap stay yours to list. Docker's reasons say what is wrong in plain words first (`Docker isn't running on this machine`), then what to do.
  The box image's first build is said in the terminal as it starts (`Preparing the Docker box image (first start only, a few minutes); agents can be made now, and their first run waits for it.`) and as it ends (`The Docker box image is ready.`). `npx @parcha/agentrun` now prints the `Boxes:` line and these two on stderr, after its `AgentRun is running at` line, so stdout stays that one line.
  `@parcha/pi-transport`, where the boxes' providers live, is now a dependency of `@parcha/agentrun-serve` rather than an optional peer, so a plain install (`npx @parcha/agentrun` included) has the Docker box.
- `npx @parcha/agentrun` now tells the person at the terminal how to stop it and where its data is kept: "Press Ctrl-C to stop it. Its data is in ~/.agentrun." (or the directory `--data` names), on stderr, so the one line on stdout that scripts read is unchanged. The command also reads `--data=<dir>` as it reads `--data <dir>`, and the last one given wins, as on the server, which is handed exactly one: before, `--data=<dir>` sent the server two and the command named the wrong one. It also says where the API's bearer token is (`<data>/.token`, or the `AGENTRUN_TOKEN` it was started with): the server's own line saying so stays out of this terminal.

  Started with `AGENTRUN_PUBLIC_URL` (a server behind HTTPS on its own name), the command now starts: it takes the server's "Open https://<that name>/" line, where before it waited 60 seconds for a 127.0.0.1 one and stopped the server. A server that does not start is shown with its own last lines ("AgentRun did not start. The server's last lines: …"), where before the message pointed at lines this terminal never showed. A stop the command passes on (Ctrl-C, a process manager's SIGTERM) now ends it with 0, so `systemctl stop` no longer logs a failure.
- A run whose learning kept nothing for a reason (the grader turned it away, a check refused it, it was skipped or stopped) says so on its page: a "Kept nothing" chip with the server's own sentence, linking to the agent's Learning tab. Before, only the run's Timeline said so. A run with nothing new to remember stays silent. The run's look-back line and the Learning tab's panel of runs that kept nothing (now titled "Kept nothing") show the server's sentence as it is, so one that names a lost skill says "Not kept" once.
- Tests only: the CLI test that starts a server with accounts off and a public URL lets the server pick its own port (`--port 0`) and reads it from the start line, so another process can no longer take the port between the test choosing it and the server listening.
- The source check gives the downloaded source tree's install and whole build 600 seconds each, where every command had 180: a slow CI runner ran the build past 180 seconds once. Every other command keeps 180.

## 0.1.0-beta.30 (2026-11-05)

- The console's agent editor sets an agent's default spend cap. Settings has "Default spend cap per run, $" on an agentic agent (not a workflow one): it shows the revision's `max_cost_usd`, and a save sends the number typed. Emptying the field clears a saved cap with an explicit `max_cost_usd: 0`, the server's "no default"; an edit never clears it by leaving the field out. Text that is no number is refused under the field and not sent; the server's own answer on the number shows under it too. A new agent's Create carries the cap typed. An edit keeps every definition field the form does not show.
- The console raises a run's spend cap. On a run paused at its cap, beside the cap's banner, the new cap is prefilled with the server's suggestion (`pending.suggested_cap_usd`), editable, and "Raise the cap and continue" sends it once (`PATCH /v2/runs/{id}`); the run is read again and goes on. The run header offers "Raise the cap" for any capped agentic run that has not ended, so a working run can be raised before it pauses; its answer's note (the live process keeps its old cap until its next one) is shown. A refusal is said under the input in the server's words (422), and an ended run (409) or a run the server no longer has (404) in plain words. The Report tab of a paused run reads "Paused at its spend cap", with what it spent of its cap.
- The console's Learning tab retires a learned skill or a workflow draft as it retires a note. An active or waiting skill and a draft workflow have Retire, behind the notes' confirm in place ("Retire it? Later runs stop getting it. It stays under Earlier skills."), made on the listing's version of its `SKILL.md` or its draft's `README.md` (never its `workflow.json`). A retired one moves under Earlier skills or Earlier workflows, closed until asked, with no Approve and no Retire, and is no longer counted as in use. A version changed since the listing (412) says "It changed since you opened it. Reload the tab to see the newest version.", as a note does.
- The console's runs list names the schedule that started a run, as the run's page does, with a link to the schedule: "Schedule: <name>", or its id when the schedule is not in the list.
- The console has a Schedules page, where the server has schedules (`features.triggers`). It lists each schedule: its name, its agent, when it fires (a cron line and its zone, one time, or an interval), its next time, whether it is paused, and how its last firing went. A schedule is made or edited in a form: when (cron in an IANA zone, one time, or every N seconds, minutes or hours from an optional start), the agent, what each run is asked, and its required cost cap. A save the server refuses shows each issue under the field it names, a workflow agent's refusal under the agent. An edit sends only what changed. A schedule's page pauses and resumes it, edits it, deletes it after a confirm, and runs it now, one firing per click under its own Idempotency-Key. It lists its firings, newest first: started (with its run), skipped, missed (with the count), failed (with why), or due. A run a schedule started names the schedule on its page, and links to it.
- **The console answers a tool call that waits for your approval, and sets each tool's permission**
  - A run waiting on a tool call set to ask (`pending.kind` `approval`) shows an approval card on its page where a question would be. The card names the tool and shows its arguments in a monospace block that scrolls, and never runs wider than a phone. It says what a call costs ("Costs $0.02 per call", "Not metered", or nothing when the tool names no price) and takes an optional note, sent with Deny. Allow and Deny both disable on the first click, so a double click is one answer. The run is read again after the answer. A refused answer is said in words, and the words stay after the card goes: another answer came first, the run was stopped (the call withdrawn) or cancelled, the page has no owner sign-in, or the sign-in ended. While the call waits, the message box stays and says what a send does: a message declines the call, and the agent reads it as the reason ("or type what to do instead; sending it declines this call"). The card goes once the run is read again, as after a Deny, and the timeline's row reads "Declined by your message". An answered question that is still the newest on the timeline is no longer shown as the question.
  - Mission Control's Needs you strip answers the same call in place, with a compact card under the agent, the run and when it asked.
  - The timeline's `tool_approval_requested` row reads "Your approval", marked while the call waits, and points to the card above.
  - Settings → Tools lists every tool with its permission (`allow`, `ask`, `deny`) beside its provider, with the owner's default named. The agent editor does the same for each tool the agent grants, the server's permission its default. Where accounts are off (`ask_needs: "password"`), ask is offered disabled with why, and a tool already on ask says each call is refused. Saving a provider or a permission keeps every other setting the server holds, a catalog tool's or an MCP server's wildcard included: `PUT /v2/settings/tools` replaces the whole map.
  - A workflow refused at its start because a call node calls a tool set to deny, or to ask (a call node can't wait for an approval), says the server's words and links to the fix: "Set <tool> for this agent" opens the agent's Tools on that tool's permission (`#/agents/<id>/edit?tool=<tool>`, a legacy `fetch_page` at `web_fetch`'s), or Settings → Tools for every agent. It reads `details.denied` and `details.asks` on the agent page and in the Playground. A workflow agent's editor now shows the permission of each tool its call nodes call (the server's built-in call tools, no catalog tool, no provider), saved in its `tool_settings` with the workflow kept whole.
- Console: the file viewer shows a file Rendered or Raw, with a toggle in its header. Rendered is the Markdown rendering it showed before; Raw is the file's exact text, each line on its own line, in monospace. A Markdown file opens Rendered and any other text file Raw, so `Hello, world!` and `Goodbye, world!` on two lines of a `.txt` read as two lines, and a `.md`'s are one click from it. A file the viewer doesn't show (an image, a binary) is still offered as a download, with no toggle.
- The console's timeline shows a message the server wrote into a run (an approval's resume, a raised cap's follow-up: a `phase` row with `origin: "server"`) as AgentRun's: labelled "AgentRun", its first line as the title without the "AgentRun:" prefix, muted. It never reads as the person's. A plain phase row keeps "Phase".
- The agent's Analytics tab no longer shows a run that just ended as unknown (no effort, 0 tokens, cost Unknown) until a reload. It reads its figures again when it opens, when a run of the agent that was going changes status (followed on its timeline, as the Runs tab follows its latest run), and a few times more, briefly, while the server's `runs.status_unknown` says a run's end is not in its store yet. Mission Control's agent picker now scopes everything below it: with an agent picked, "Runs per agent" shows that agent's row alone and "Recent runs" that agent's runs alone; with All agents both list every agent, as before. Its Needs input tile now counts exactly the runs Needs you lists, those with a pending ask; a run the person stopped is no longer counted there, and shows in Recent runs as Stopped with "send a message to continue".
- The console shows what an agent wrote as it wrote it. A single line break in an answer, a note or a file is a line break: an answer asked for in three lines reads as three lines, where it read as one. Lists, code blocks and paragraphs are unchanged. The timeline's Answer row, which shows the answer's first line, ends it with "…" when the answer has more. Text in angle brackets that is no HTML the console keeps reads literally: "Customer: <name>" and "Amount: $<amount>" in an answer, a learned note or a skill, where the tag was dropped and they read "Customer: " and "Amount: $". A `<script>` an agent quotes reads as its text and never runs. HTML the console keeps (`<b>`, `<br>`, `<img>`, a table and its cells) is still HTML, and a template whose first line is `<name>` keeps its lines, and the sanitizer still checks everything.
- A link in a run's answer no longer takes the console's tab away. A relative link that names one of the run's files (an agent that wrote `hello.md` and answered with `[hello.md](hello.md)`) opens that file in the run's file viewer, and a Markdown file in the viewer opens the run files it links, read from its own folder. Any other link with no scheme (a path that names no run file, a path from the root, a fragment, `//host`) reads as its text: before, the browser followed it against the console's address, onto the server's JSON 404 or, for a fragment, onto another console page. A link no URL parser takes reads as its text too, and a folder name with `#`, `?` or `%` in it is read as a name. A link to a page (`http:`, `https:`) opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`; before, it replaced the console. The same holds wherever the console renders Markdown: a learning note, the agent editor's preview. A `mailto:` or `tel:` link is unchanged.
- The browser's provider choice, in Settings → Tools and in an agent's editor, names and explains the browser's providers: "Auto (Browserbase where its key is saved, else Kernel)", "Browserbase", "Kernel" and "Local Chrome", each with a line on what it needs. It read them through the names web_search and web_fetch share, so Kernel read "kernel fetch" with the guarded fetch's help, auto took search's rule, and Browserbase and chrome showed their ids. What the browser resolves to reads by the same names. Search's and fetch's names and help are unchanged.
- Console: a banner above a page (no model yet, an import under way, a failed sign-out) now takes its own space above the page instead of sitting inside it, so a full-height page keeps its foot in view: the agent editor's Previous, Next and Create no longer drop below the window while "No model yet" shows. A harness card this server can't run says "Not available here", and the server's whole reason, with its install command, opens in a "Why, and what to do" line that wraps inside the card, on a phone as on a desktop, where it ran out of the narrow card before.
- The console's first-time approval path reads plainer. The approval card's button says "Allow once", on the run page and in Mission Control, since the next call asks again. A call's arguments show as one "key: value" line per argument (a string as it is, a nested value as JSON), with the whole arguments on hover. A preview the server cut shows as it came. In the agent editor, a tool's description stays at two lines with "more" for the rest, where it had shown whole because the clamp was overridden. Settings → Tools puts each tool's permission right after its name: Tool, Permission, Provider, Resolves to.
- Console on a phone on its side (844 by 390) and other short or narrow screens: the sidebar scrolls, so Sign out at its foot is always reachable; the agent editor's tool cards size their columns by a minimum width, one card a row in a narrow pane instead of two tall strips; and the harness cards wrap onto more rows instead of squeezing five into one, so their names no longer run into each other.
- Console: every time it shows (a run's header, the Runs list, the Timeline, a run's files, learned notes, Mission Control, an agent's runs and revisions, tool keys' checks and the webhook's deliveries) reads in the viewer's own time zone, the browser's, instead of UTC. A run started at 20:50 UTC now reads 4:50 PM to a viewer in UTC-4. All of them go through one formatter, so the webhook's deliveries now read in the same short shape as the rest. Relative times and the analytics' days, which are the server's UTC days, are unchanged.
- The console on a phone. On a narrow portrait screen the Agents search box shows its magnifier without the placeholder (only its first letter fitted); landscape and desktop keep it. The Playground's Agent and Revision fields are a picker that opens its list right under the field (Headless UI's Listbox, `@headlessui/react` 2.2.10), in place of native selects, which a phone opened detached from the field. In a short viewport (a phone in landscape) the Playground's code and response panel folds behind Show code so the form gets the height; the agent header stays put and Send request sits in its own row. Sending opens the panel so the response shows.
- Console: an agent's Learning tab, under "What the next run reads", counts and names the agent's active skills beside the notes in use: "1 skill", "2 notes · 1 skill", and, opened, "Active skills: find-policies". An agent whose only learning was an approved skill read "nothing" there, though later runs get that skill. A skill that waits for its owner's OK is still left out, and with learning off (for the agent, or on the server) the panel says the next run gets no notes or skills. A long skill name wraps inside the panel.
- The console's Report tab never says "Writing the answer" for a run that isn't working on one. A run waiting on a question reads "Waiting for your answer: the run continues when you reply below." (or "once the question is answered" where the page takes no answer); a stopped run reads "Stopped before it wrote an answer. Send a message below to continue it."; a paused workflow run reads "Paused: no new step starts until it's resumed." A run that waits on a kind of thing the console doesn't know yet (its `pending.kind`, from a newer server) reads "Waiting for you: the run continues once you give it what it waits on." Only a queued or working run reads "Writing the answer". A failed, cancelled or answerless completed run already says how it ended, and still does.
- The back arrow before a run page's breadcrumb goes back: to the console page the run was opened from (the runs list, the agent's page), or to the run's agent page when the run was opened at its own address, from outside the console. It was an icon that did nothing. The breadcrumb's agent name links to the agent's page.
- A workflow agent's Tool settings in the console say what ask means for it: a call node can't wait for an approval, so a tool set to ask stops the run from starting, and the fix is to set it to allow. The line had said a tool on ask makes the run wait for your approval at each call, which holds for an agentic run only, and still says so there.
- A decision receipt keeps its request's error whole up to 64 KB (it was cut at 2000 characters), marking a longer one `error_truncated`. A workflow run's graph redacts a decision's error before it cuts it, and holds back the tail of one stored cut (marked, or exactly 2000 characters from before), as it does a call's, so a held key across the cut shows no part.
- A decision's row in a run's store keeps its receipt's `error_truncated`, so a completed run's decision error stored cut at 64 KB is still held back at its tail on the graph, where a held value the cut split would sit.
- The test shards' weights (`scripts/ci-timings.json`) are refreshed from 168 job logs of three green pull-request runs; the old ones dated from the day sharding landed. The root scripts suite had grown (`release-preflight.test.mjs` alone went from 152 s to 257 s), and all of it sat on `any-2`, which then ran 7.8 min against the other `any` shards' 5.4 to 6.1 and held up `lean` and `verify` in 36 of 40 runs. Each shard now prints `ci-shard: <shard> ran <s> s, modelled <s> s` and, when it ran more than a quarter and more than a minute over its model, a `::warning` that the weights are stale, with the command that refreshes them.
- A flaky test can be quarantined: skipped on CI only, under its flake id and owner, for at most 24 hours, with at most three at once (`node scripts/quarantine.mjs --add`, `scripts/quarantine.json`). Once an entry expires, `scripts/quarantine.test.mjs` fails every pull request until the test is fixed and its entry removed. CONTRIBUTING.md says how.
- Greptile reviews every push to a pull request, not only its opening (`greptile.json`, `"triggerOnUpdates": true`). A push that adds commits is reviewed as it lands, so nobody has to ask for another review. Every other review setting stays as the Greptile dashboard has it.
- serve's test of OpenCode on one port across twenty drives no longer fails now and then on a busy CI machine. It took its port from the range the kernel hands to any listen(0) or outbound connection, so another test could be handed that port in the moment between one drive and the next, and the next OpenCode could not bind it ("OpenCode exited before it listened", ServeError). The test now takes a free port below that range, and a drive that does not complete names its error. This ejected a merge-queue run and failed a PR on 11-05 (F36); its quarantine is lifted.
- `scripts/release-preflight.test.mjs` packs its fixture packages side by side, at most one per CPU, instead of one after another: 18 `npm pack` runs, 13 times in the file, which took most of its 257 s on CI and held up the test shard it sat on. On the shared box the file went from 330 s to 220 s; the receipt it writes is unchanged, in the release map's order.
- The release sets `@parcha/agentrun`'s version; a pull request no longer bumps it. A change to what the `npx @parcha/agentrun` command publishes now adds only a fragment for `@parcha/agentrun`, and `node scripts/changes.mjs --check` refuses a pull request that moves its version. On the release pull request, which moves the command's pin on the server every time, the check still refuses a change to its published files that keeps the version, so each release gives it the next `x.y.z`. Pull requests stop colliding on that number, and the changelog stops naming versions that were never published (0.3.1 to 0.3.3 in beta.29). `docs/releasing.md` says so.
- serve's dwf test of a run's graph read while it is paused mid-map bounds each read at 10 s, not 1 s. A read that waited on the paused run's store would wait until the resume, and one that met a busy store answers at once marked stale (still asserted), so the bound only clears a slow runner: one answered in 2.6 s and ejected a merge-queue run on 11-05 (F30).
- serve's tests read the fake docker's logs by whole lines only: a log just made and still empty, or a last line still being written, is no longer parsed, so the box default-image test no longer fails with "Unexpected end of JSON input" on a busy runner (F35). Its CI quarantine is lifted.
- serve's secret-custody test reads a live run's files while the run goes on, and a file that goes between its listing and its read (the run's empty launch mark, removed once its process serves) no longer fails the test with ENOENT. Such a file holds nothing; any other read error still fails, and a file that holds a key is still found. This ejected a merge-queue run on 11-04 (F31).
- serve's store-busy test of a message to a working run bounds each answer at half the revision read's retry (15 s of 30 s), not at 6 s: CI's slowest runners answered at 6.0 to 6.5 s and failed it on four pull requests and a merge-queue run on 11-05 (F34), while a message that waited on the read would answer at 30 s. A server whose message path makes that read still fails it, at 30 s.
- The dependency inventory (docs/dependencies.md) and the release notices read OpenCode's twelve platform binaries (opencode-darwin-arm64@1.19.5 to opencode-windows-x64-baseline@1.19.5) as MIT, from OpenCode's own LICENSE at the pinned release, instead of "UNKNOWN — Missing license metadata": their package manifests name no license. The reading is pinned by name and version, so a bumped binary is flagged again until it is checked. The release review's flags drop from 21 to 9.

## 0.1.0-beta.29 (2026-11-04)

- Known issue: with a Jev key set (`TYPESAFE_API_KEY`), a note that records a preference you stated can be dropped; fixed in the next beta.
- The DSL's command line is `agentrun-dsl`, no longer `agentrun`: `agentrun-dsl demo`, `agentrun-dsl inspect workflow.json`, `agentrun-dsl dry-run`, and so on, with the same commands and options. A script or shell that calls `agentrun` from `@parcha/agentrun-dsl` calls `agentrun-dsl` instead. The `agentrun` name is left for the AgentRun server.
- **A host with no decision receipts now escalates instead of re-asking.** A host that runs decisions under `withRecovery` without `recordDecision` and `recovery.decision`, and whose process died after a decision's answer and before its node committed, used to ask the decision again and pay for it twice. It now ends the run escalated, kind `decision_interrupted` (exported as `DECISION_INTERRUPTED`), stage `decision`, for review. To keep the run going instead, keep decision receipts (`recordDecision` plus `recovery.decision`): an answered decision then replays from its receipt at no cost.

  Under `withRecovery`, a decision is never paid for twice across a crash. Each request to the host's `runJudge` is committed as a `decision.request` before the judge is asked, and ends with one outcome: `answered`, or `failed` in its own process (asked again by the next as its next attempt). A later process that finds a request asked and never answered (its process died, or a cancel cut the call) does not ask again: it books the request `interrupted` at an unknown cost (`cost_usd: null`, never 0) and ends the run escalated as above. A pause now waits for a decision in flight to finish, as it waits for an effect.
  - The driver keeps the requests in its frame by node path (`decisions`, optional in older checkpoints), so a map item's requests live in its own record in the keyed layout. `driver.decisionRequests()` reads them back.
- **`createJevRunner` never re-sends a paid request unless you ask it to**
  - A public default that could re-send a paid request was the bug. A request that was sent may have been answered and billed, so the runner no longer retries a 408, a 5xx or a connection lost after sending. It sends the request once and fails with the new `JevError` code `outcome_unknown` (with the HTTP `status` when there is one), at an unknown cost.
  - It still retries a request that provably never reached Jev (no DNS answer, no connection, no TLS handshake), and a 429 with `Retry-After`. A 429 without `Retry-After` is now an `http` error at once.
  - Who breaks: a host that counted on the runner retrying 5xx responses, lost connections or 429s with no `Retry-After`. What to do: hosts that want the old retries opt in with `createJevRunner({ retryAfterSend: true })`, knowing such a retry can re-send a request Jev answered and billed. A host that switches on `JevError.code` handles `outcome_unknown`, for example by sending the case to a person rather than asking again.
- `npx @parcha/agentrun` installs the server it was released with: `@parcha/agentrun-serve` is its one dependency, at the release's exact version, so npx installs the two together and nothing is fetched at run time (no `@latest`). The command passes its arguments through, with its store (`--data ~/.agentrun` unless `--data` names another), and the server's CLI picks what it used to: with no `--port`, `agentrun-serve` takes the first free port from 7340, found by listening, so a port another process takes at the same moment is passed over (`--port N` is still exact and fails at once when taken, `0` any); `--open` opens the console's page in the browser once the server listens (never the banner's sign-in link, so the token stays out of the opener's arguments and the browser's history), except in CI or on Linux with no display (the command passes it unless `--no-open`). The CLI sets no model: with no `AGENT_MODEL`, the server's default is the first provider key it has, set where it starts or saved in the console's Settings → Model keys, so a key saved later moves the default with no restart. The release publishes the command after the server, and `release-preflight` refuses a command whose server pin is not the release version, so every release bumps the command too. This release publishes `@parcha/agentrun` 0.3.4.
- `npx @parcha/agentrun` starts AgentRun on this machine with one command: it checks Node, takes the first free port from 7340 on the loopback address, keeps its data in `~/.agentrun`, starts the server, waits until it answers, prints on one line the address the server says to open (the URL of its `Open` line) and opens it (not with `--no-open`, in CI, or with no display). With `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` set and no `AGENT_MODEL`, the server runs a current model of that provider. Other commands go to the server's CLI: `npx @parcha/agentrun import`. The TypeScript SDK for the hosted API stays `@parcha/agentrun` 0.2.x; a dependency on `^0.2` never gets 0.3.0. The release publishes `@parcha/agentrun` at its own version, first, and `changes --check` fails a change to the files it ships that keeps its version. The README's first run is two lines, Docker and Render move under "Run it on a server", and `docs/SKILL.md` is the setup skill for a coding agent.
- The console's agent page opens on the agent's latest run, above its runs: its status, its title, when it started, its timeline as the run page shows it, and a link to the whole run. While the run goes, its timeline follows it from the cursor, and when the run's status moves, the run is read again, so its badge and its row in the list move together. A read that fails is tried again, even after the timeline's last page; one that keeps failing says the run could not be refreshed, with a Retry, rather than show the old status. An agent with no runs says so, and that its first run will show there. The page reads what it read before (the agent's runs, the run's timeline and the run), with no new server route.
- The console gives each agent its own page at `#/agents/{id}`. The header shows the current revision's harness, model (the server's default, named as such, when the revision sets none), thinking and revision number, and says when the agent is archived. Each tab has its own address (`#/agents/{id}/{tab}`). The Runs tab, the default, lists the agent's runs newest first, an archived agent's included. Each row links to its run and shows its status, when it started, how long it ran once ended, and its cost once known. A workflow agent also has a Workflow tab that draws the current revision's graph read-only. The editor moves to `#/agents/{id}/edit`, reached from the page's Edit button and the card's Edit icon. A card and a newly created agent open the page, and the card's History icon and Mission Control's runs per agent open its Runs tab. `#/runs?agent={id}` still lists one agent's runs.
- An agent's page in the console runs the agent and shows its analytics. A run bar under the header takes an agentic agent's first message, or a workflow agent's input as JSON (prefilled with its example, else its fields empty), and an effort from the server's `features.efforts` ("default" sends none). A started run opens on its page; a refusal (a `409 agent_not_runnable`, a `422` with its fields) is shown in the bar, and sending again is the same request under the same `Idempotency-Key`. An archived agent has no run bar. The Runs tab shows each run's effort, and an Analytics tab, on a server whose `GET /v2/stats` answers `analytics`, shows the agent's tokens, time, success, cost, success by harness and effort, top tools, and runs and tokens per day over the last 7 or 30 UTC days or every run, drawn with Mission Control's analytics components: a token sum says how many runs it holds, unmetered requests are named, and a cost not known is never $0.
  - serve's runs index (`GET /v2/runs`, `GET /v2/agents/{id}/runs`) now carries a run's `effort` as `GET /v2/runs/{id}` does: optional, absent for a run started with none, marked `effort/1` in the served OpenAPI document. The document also lists every other field a run answer carries beyond the contract, marked `serve/1`: `revision`, `kind` and `archived_at` on `Run` and `RunSummary`, and `error` on `RunSummary`. Before, a run or a runs-index answer did not meet the document's closed schemas.
- The console's agents: the list (and the archived ones) and an editor for a new agent or the next revision of one. The editor has context, tools and skills, settings (the harness from the server's `harnesses`, a harness it does not run shown disabled with its reason; thinking; model) and history (make an earlier revision current), plus archive and unarchive. A save is made on the revision the editor loaded (`If-Match`). When another save landed first, the editor says so and keeps nothing over it. A 422 shows its fields where they are. An edit keeps every definition field the form does not show, and the name and description the agent was created with.
- `apps/console` is the start of AgentRun Server's console: a React app that builds to static files, with the shell and the run page (status, live timeline, input required, stop versus cancel, files and the file viewer, typed errors) and a Storybook of every component on responses recorded from the server. It offers a message only in a mode the server's `features.messages` takes. The source export now reviews `.tsx` files, and CI's `verify` needs the console's typecheck, tests and builds.
- The console says how much a run whose requests were cut can have cost. Where a run's cost is unknown but the server bounds it (`at_most_usd`, set only when every cut request has a recorded figure), the run header and the run rows read "≤ $x" with "some requests were cut", in place of "Unknown". A bound is rounded up, so it never reads below itself, and is never a bare "$x". Mission Control's total cost and the analytics' cost keep the known sum and add "up to $y more for N cut runs" from `/v2/stats`' `bounded_unknown` (the analytics' under their range and agent). A server without these fields reads exactly as before.
- The console shows what a run has cost so far when its whole cost is unknown. The run header, the runs list, an agent's runs, Mission Control's latest runs and the live runs under Needs you read "so far $0.0123 · 1 not priced", from the server's `so_far_usd` and `unpriced`, in place of "Unknown". It says "est." for a local estimate, and the run header's hover says it is not the whole cost. "Unknown" stays for a run whose server says neither. The console's reads of a cost bound, which no server sends, are removed: `at_most_usd`, `/v2/stats`' `bounded_unknown`, and the "≤ $x" and "up to $y more for N cut runs" lines.
- The console's agent builder can create a decision agent: on a new agent, the harness picker lists **Decision** after Pi, Claude and Codex. Picking it makes the agent a workflow revision built from a starter decision workflow (one typed question, and a person for an unclear or low-confidence answer, adapted from the repository's jury demo; it has no code node, so a server started without `--allow-code-nodes` runs it), whose question goes to the server's decision provider: Jev, the default, or Parallel where Jev does not run, with no instructions, tools, thinking or model to fill in; after Create the agent opens on its Workflow step, where its nodes are edited. Decision is offered when the server's capabilities list the `runJudge` workflow adapter and Jev or Parallel among the decision providers available, and shown disabled with why otherwise, so the console never makes a decision agent whose run the server would refuse. After Decision the picker lists **Workflow**, offered on every server: it makes a workflow revision from a general starter, one agent step that does what its input's `task` asks and answers in `answer` (no code node, no decision node, an example input), again with no instructions, tools, thinking or model to fill in, and opens on the Workflow step, where its JSON is edited or another workflow's pasted over it. So a server with no decision provider can still create a workflow agent from the console. The harness picker shows one column per choice on a wide screen. Both are choices in the console only: the revision names no harness, and the server is unchanged.
- The console's start screens take files. The Playground and the agent page's run bar each have a file picker, a button and a drop zone, that sends each picked file at once (`POST /v2/files?path=<its name>`), all in parallel, with its size and how much is sent. The run names them (`file_ids`) and finds them in `inputs/`; it starts only once each is sent. The server's limits from capabilities (`limits.max_upload_bytes`, `limits.max_files_per_run`) are said under the picker and kept before anything is sent, and a refusal the server gives is said there in its words. Removing a file stops its send, or deletes it from the server when it was sent.
- An agent whose harness the server can't run says why on its page, in place of a run that would fail: "claude-agent doesn't run on this server: claude-agent needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.", from capabilities' `harnesses[].reason`, or the harness by name when the server gives no reason. Its Run is off and sends nothing.
- The console shows what an agent learns. The agent page has a Learning tab: each note the agent wrote for itself, with its type, its body, the run it was learned in and, on a server that says what runs read, how many runs used it. A person edits a note in place (name, description and type as fields, the body as markdown; Ctrl or ⌘+Enter saves, Esc cancels), adds one, or retires one after a confirm. Each write names the version the person saw (the note's own ETag on its card), so a note changed in the meantime is never written over or retired unseen: the newest version loads, and the person's edit stays on screen to copy. Notes that were replaced or retired are listed under Earlier notes. Beside them are the learning switch, the latest runs that kept nothing with the reason in plain words, what the next run reads, and, from a server that has it, the before and after ("Since it learned (on 4 runs): answers in 12s instead of 18s · $0.03 est. instead of $0.11 est. a run"); until it has two runs on each side of the first note, one quiet line says so. An agent with no notes shows one sentence on what learning is, with the switch. The editor's Settings step has a "Learns from its runs" switch. It asks for no tool, since a run says what it learned in its answer. A run's header and its rows on the agent page and Mission Control say "Used 1 note" and "Learned 1 note", naming the notes on hover; the header's chips open the notes in the Learning tab. An ended run whose learning is still deciding is read again until it settles, and its timeline is read again each time its learning moves, so the timeline says what the header says. From a server that holds notes for the owner's OK (a note that changes what the agent trusts or skips), those notes sit at the top of the tab under "Waiting for your OK", each with Retire and, from a server that approves from the console, Approve, made on the card's version (a quiet line says when approving needs the owner's password sign-in, or comes in the next update), beside a line on what a run on the host can reach; a note from the person reads "From you", and a run that kept one says "1 note waits for your OK", linking to it. In the timeline, the end-of-run look-back is one step labelled Learning that says what it kept ("Looked back: kept 2 notes (Refund limit, Answer shape)", "Looked back: kept 1 note (Answer in tables); 1 note waits for your OK (Buy from PaperDepot24 first)", or "Looked back: nothing new to keep"), with what it did behind it; its message is no longer labelled as the person's. A run whose notes came back out of format says so: "Not kept: its notes weren't in the expected format." A run that kept a note shows "What it changed" under its report and its timeline: each note its learning added, replaced, retired or removed, by name ("Added: Refund limit", "Replaced: Old refund rule → Refund limit"), each opening to its text before and after with the changed lines marked.
- The console reads a live AgentRun Server: the runs, and a run followed from its timeline cursor (long poll), with stop, cancel and messages sent as same-origin JSON with an `Idempotency-Key`. `npm run dev` in `apps/console` proxies `/v2` to a local server (`AGENTRUN_URL`, `AGENTRUN_TOKEN`).
- Mission Control shows a run's tokens, time, tools and success, not only counts and cost, when the server answers `GET /v2/stats` with `analytics`. A server without it shows the page as before.
  - Tiles for tokens (with the cached share of input and the output), run time (wall time of ended runs, and active time where the metrics have it), success rate (completed against failed or escalated) and cost. When some runs have no metrics, the tokens say so ("tokens from 9 of 12 runs"), and the requests that went uncounted are named.
  - A per-day trend of the range, at most the last 30 days: runs stacked as completed, failed and other, and tokens, each bar with a readout on hover and focus, plus a table view. Success, tokens and cost by harness and effort, and the top tools with their error rate. Imported runs are counted apart from the ones run here.
  - A range picker (7 days, 30 days, all) and an agent picker, which ask the server again. A cost no run reported reads Unknown, never $0.
- The console says when no model can run. The first screen asks for a model key (a card that saves one through the key routes) while the server's capabilities say `models.ready: false`, and every other page shows a bar with what to set: a model provider's key where the server starts, or one added in Settings, or, when the server's `AGENT_MODEL` names a model it does not run, that. While no model is ready, the console reads the capabilities again every 10 s and when the page comes back into view, so the card and the bar go by themselves.
- The console sets and shows where an agent runs. The editor's Settings step picks On the server or In a sandbox, with a box from the server's list; a box the server lacks is greyed with its reason. Picking a box opens bash, and going back to the server turns bash off and says so. The agent page says where the agent runs, and the run page where the run ran. A start refused for a missing box says which box. A tool the server can't run shows the server's reason, and a refusal at one tool shows on the Tools field.
- The console waits out a server that goes away (a restart, a laptop waking up) instead of showing the password page or "TypeError: Failed to fetch". Reads wait and carry on once the server answers, so a page keeps what it shows and a run's timeline picks up where it was. One banner says it is reconnecting, with the next try and Retry now, then "Resumed". Errors say what failed, why and what to do next, a missing run or agent links to its list, and pages show their shape while loading.
- The console's agent History has **View** on each of a workflow agent's past revisions. It opens that revision's workflow as its graph, read-only and marked against the current revision (new, edited, removed, moved), with **Make current** beside it and **Back to History**. A read-only view takes no edit, asks the server for no check, and has no save. An agentic agent's History is unchanged.
- The console archives runs and filters the runs list, as the server's own page does. A run's page has **Archive** (or **Unarchive**) in its header, and an archived run shows an Archived chip there. The runs list has a state filter and an **Archived** toggle that lists only archived runs. The address keeps both (`#/runs?status=failed&archived=true`, beside `agent=`), so a reload keeps the view. The client's `archiveRun` and `unarchiveRun` send the empty JSON body a cookie write needs, and `listRuns` and `listAgentRuns` take `{ status, archived }`.
- The console's setup offers to import this machine's Claude Code, Codex and pi sessions. On a server on the same machine, the first-visit card shows the box at once, never ticked for you. It reads "Counting sessions…" until the server's numbers arrive, and the count isn't known if counting failed. Ticked, setup asks the server to import them once the password is set. The console then shows the import running and then done, and it reads the agents' names again once the import ends. Where the server offers no import, there is no box.
- The console signs in through AgentRun Server's own routes (`/v2/auth/session`, `/v2/auth/sign-in`, `/v2/setup`, `/v2/auth/sign-out`): the first visit sets the owner's password, later visits sign in with it, and Sign out ends the session. The server's HttpOnly cookie is the session; the console keeps no credential. A server without accounts sends the person to the link it printed.
- The console starts runs. The Operator is one composer: pick an agent, give it its first message, and the run starts and opens. The playground is `POST /v2/agents/{id}/runs` as a request form (input, revision, effort, spend cap) or its JSON. It shows the same request as cURL and JavaScript, and the server's answer with a way into the run. A retry of the same request reuses its `Idempotency-Key`, so the server answers with the run it already started.
- The console follows the OS's light or dark scheme instead of always being dark, and follows it as it changes. A switch at the foot of the sidebar picks System, Light or Dark, and this browser remembers the pick. A dark OS's page is dark before the script runs, so it never flashes white.
- Settings → Tool keys says, on each key's row, how its provider took the saved key: "<Label> accepted this key · <time>", or "Not checked: <reason>" with the time when the server tried. It says nothing when the server sends no check. A key its provider refuses is not saved, and the server's message shows under the key's input.
- The console's Settings → Tool keys page, over `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE /v2/settings/tool-keys`: each key (Parallel, Monid, Browserbase) with what it unlocks and the harnesses that run that, and each of its fields set or not set, from the server or saved here. Add key and Change open an inline form with one password input per field the page may set; it sends only the fields typed in, then reads the keys again, and Remove asks first. A key the server is started with is read-only and says so. No value is ever shown, pre-filled or left in the page: the inputs are uncontrolled (so no value reaches a `value` attribute) and cleared after a save or a cancel. Saved keys that no longer open raise a banner naming why and which to enter again. A server with no seal key (`at_rest: "unavailable"`, or a `409 no_sealing_key`) says it can't store keys and offers no save. A 422 shows at its field. Settings is in the nav only when the server's capabilities have `features.tool_keys.routes`.
- The console sets which provider serves each tool that has a choice, such as web search and web fetch. Settings → Tools is the server's own choice: each tool's providers in the server's order, the default first, what each resolves to on each harness, and a change saved at once. A pick unlike the default is marked, and can be reset. The agent editor's Tools step sets an agent's own provider over the server's, saved with the agent's definition. It says what the provider resolves to for that agent once the change is saved.
- The console has a webhook page, Settings → Webhook (`#/settings/webhook`), on a server with the webhook routes. It shows the URL run notices are POSTed to, whether its secret signs here, and why it is off when a receiver's 410 turned it off. The signing secret is shown on the save that made it, and never again. A test send says how it went. The last attempts are listed newest first, each with the receiver's answer or why it failed and when it is tried again, and any message of the log can be redelivered under its own `webhook-id`. Settings gains a tab for each of its pages.
- The console edits a workflow agent. A workflow agent's editor opens on a Workflow step that draws its graph, with provider and model chips, the questions, and image and web badges on each decision node. Its JSON redraws the graph as you type, marks what changed against the current revision, and shows the server's validation (`POST /v2/workflows/validate`, or the console's own lint on a server without the route) at each issue's line. A refused save lists every field at its line. The Playground prefills a workflow agent's input with its schema's example (else its fields, empty), and the agents list describes a workflow agent by its shape.
- The console draws a workflow run live. A workflow run's page has a Graph view that polls `GET /v2/runs/{id}/graph` each second while the run goes: each node's state, the branch a route took, a map as a grid of item cells (named by document, toned by outcome, with the deciding probability), and the run's cost, decisions and elapsed time. Pause and Resume hold and release the run. Clicking a node or an item opens a drawer with the verdict (a probability bar per question, the answer chosen, the rule and the action), Parallel's cited sources, the state the model saw, the page images it was shown, the model, cost and latency, and for a call node its input and output. Written reports read as Markdown, and an ended run's header cost comes from its record. Idempotency keys no longer need `crypto.randomUUID`, so a run starts from a plain-http address too. The graph renderer is a TypeScript port of pi-durable-agent's `graph.js`, and it reads the DSL's `workflowGraph` through an `@dsl` alias.
- **Decision nodes name their provider, pin their model, ask for evidence and bound their own time**
  - `judge`, `pick`, `sift`, a judged `route` and an `ask` predicate take optional `provider`, `model`, `evidence` (`"off"`, `"if_supported"` or `"required"`) and `timeoutMs` (1 to 120000). A route by value takes none of them.
  - `judgeFromProviders({ providers, default, deadlineMs })` is the interpreter's `runJudge` over several providers: each request goes to its node's provider, or the default, with its pinned model, within the shorter of the node's `timeoutMs` and the provider's deadline. A node only ever shortens it. A call its node's bound cuts is `outcome_unknown`: never asked again, and the run escalates as `decision_interrupted`.
  - With `decisionProviders: { providers, default }` in its deps, a run checks every decision node before any node runs: a provider the host lacks, `"required"` evidence from a provider that gives none, a model it does not serve, or a question type it does not answer stops the run naming the node. A provider is looked up by own property only, so a name such as `"constructor"` is a provider the host lacks, in the check and in `judgeFromProviders`.
  - Without `decisionProviders`, a run refuses before any node runs a node that names a provider or sets `timeoutMs`: the host's judge could honour neither. `dryRunWorkflow` holds a node to no host's providers.
  - `DecisionCapabilities` takes an optional `models`, the names a node may pin (absent or null: any). A pin outside them is refused before any node runs, and `checkCapabilities` refuses it as `invalid_request` before a provider sends anything. `parallelProvider()` declares `parallel-fast`, the one mode it asks.
  - Pi's Jev judge is `judgeFromProviders` over a Jev-only registry, which the run's checks get too: a node naming another provider is refused before the run, with nothing sent to Jev, and a node's `timeoutMs` bounds its request. A Jev failure behind it keeps pi's fixed report (`jev_http` with its status, `jev_invalid_response` with its reason): `jevProvider` keeps the `JevError` as the `cause` of the `DecisionProviderError` it throws.
  - The provider's evidence (`{ url, title, excerpts, scope }`) travels with the answer to the `<as>$answers` sidecar, the decision receipt and the node's answered event; `"off"` drops it, and `"required"` with none fails the decision. Receipts name the provider that answered. A `sift` gives each item's sidecar the evidence of the request that answered it, and its answered event names the provider and every request's evidence.
  - A pinned `provider` or `model` is part of the decision id, so changing it asks the decision again. A node that pins neither keeps the id it had.
  - A decision whose outcome was lost (`outcome_unknown`) is booked with the provider's `cost_basis` beside its cost: the exchange `recordDecision` gets carries both.
  - `dryRunWorkflow` passes a node whose `evidence` is `"required"`: its synthetic answer carries a placeholder source.
- **Decision nodes send images, receipts keep what the judge saw, and a map collects its items' failed decisions**
  - `judge`, `pick`, `sift`, a judged `route` and an `ask` predicate take an optional `images: { "<token>": <reference> }`. The token must appear literally in the node's `state` or in a question's text; the reference is written as a state value is (`"{item.pages.0.image}"`) and resolves to an image path the host reads. The schema, `inspectWorkflow`, `workflowGraph` and the author contract know the field.
  - `judgeFromProvider(provider, { deadlineMs, loadImage })` and `judgeFromProviders({ ..., loadImage })` read a request's images through the host's `loadImage(ref, { signal })` before anything is sent. A provider without `image` in its inputs, a host with no `loadImage`, or an image that cannot be read is refused, and no request is made. `DecisionRequest.images` carries `{ bytes, mime, ref }` by token.
  - A decision receipt names each image as `{ token, ref, sha256, mime, bytes }`, never its bytes, and keeps the state the judge saw (`state`, past 64 KB of JSON its first 64 KB with `state_truncated: true`) and its `cost_basis`.
  - `imageDimensions(bytes)` reads a PNG's or JPEG's width and height from its header. `jevProvider` takes image input on TypeSafe's own API (as Jev assets, with the `TypeSafe-Danger-Zone-Images` header) and refuses an image over 1568 px on a side before the paid call, the largest Jev answers.
  - `jevProvider` sends a request again only on a 429 with `Retry-After` or a connect error before sending (up to `maxAttempts`, default 3). A 408, a 5xx or a connection lost after sending stays `outcome_unknown`, never sent twice.
  - Inside a `map` with `onItemFailure: "collect"`, a decision whose provider fails (rate limit, HTTP error, invalid answer, timeout) fails that item, and one whose outcome was lost, or that the provider refused, ends the item `needs_review`. The other items go on; before, any of these failed the run.
- **Decision models plug in through one seam, and Parallel Decisions is the second one after Jev**
  - `@parcha/agentrun-dsl` has `DecisionProvider`: a fast, non-agentic decision model that answers typed questions and declares what it can (question types, inputs, evidence, where its probabilities come from, web grounding, replay, limits) and the keys it reads by name. `judgeFromProvider(provider)` is a `runJudge`, so every decision node runs on it unchanged. A request beyond what the provider declares is refused before it is sent; a choice or score with probabilities but no confidence gets one derived with the formulas TypeSafe documents for Jev, and the decision receipt's new `confidence_source` names each one's source. The result also carries the provider's evidence, its request id and `cost_basis` (`reported`, `estimate` or `unknown`, which goes with a null cost).
  - A provider may refuse a question: `{ "type": "refusal" }` is a valid answer. It never decodes to a value (not false, not an option, not a score). The run ends `escalated` for a person, with kind `DECISION_REFUSED` (`"decision_refused"`) at stage `"decision"` and the workflow state at the node, so the case is kept instead of lost to a failed run. A route with an `unsure` branch takes that branch instead, as it would an answer of no confidence. The receipt lists the refused questions in `refused`.
  - `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/testing` has `registerDecisionProviderConformance`, the suite a provider's tests register with a fake transport: exact answers, a null cost never read as 0, typed errors (`auth`, `invalid_request`, `rate_limited`, `invalid_response`, `aborted`, and `outcome_unknown` for a 5xx or a deadline after sending from a provider that cannot replay; `http` and `timeout` for one that dedupes), no retry after sending for a provider that cannot replay, no key in what it returns or throws, declared limits refused before sending, and a refusal kept as one.
  - `@parcha/agentrun-jev` has `jevProvider(options)`, Jev as a `DecisionProvider` that passes the suite. It sends each decision once (`maxAttempts: 1`), and a 408, a 5xx or a connection error is `outcome_unknown` at an unknown cost.
  - Behaviour change under recovery (`withRecovery`): a decision whose provider throws `outcome_unknown` now ends `interrupted`, not `failed`. A failed decision is asked again by the next process; for a request sent whose outcome was lost, that would be a second paid ask. Now the next process escalates it as `decision_interrupted` without asking. Its estimate (`error.costUsd`), or an unknown cost that makes the run's spend read incomplete, is booked once, in the same commit as the outcome.
  - An answer rejected after it was paid for is booked. When `judgeFromProvider` rejects a provider's answer (`invalid_response`, for example a distribution that does not sum to 1), the error now carries the provider's cost and basis, and a provider's own `invalid_response` for a response it could not read carries its charge too: Parallel at list price, Jev at an unknown cost. The decision ends `failed` and is asked again, and its cost is booked, in the receipt and under recovery in the run's spend, with the outcome. An unknown cost counts as an unpriced request, never $0. A request refused before it was sent books nothing.
  - A provider's `outcome_unknown` error (a request sent whose outcome was lost, which may have been answered and billed) ends the run `escalated` as an interrupted decision: kind `"decision_interrupted"`, stage `"decision"`, booked at the cost the error carries. It is never asked again. The suite checks that a provider declaring `replay: "never"` never retries a failure after sending.
  - New package `@parcha/agentrun-decisions`, held (not on npm yet), with `parallelProvider()`: Parallel Decisions (`POST /v1/decisions`) with probabilities and web citations as call-level evidence. The answered request is booked at list price as an estimate. The API takes no idempotency key, so a paid request is never re-sent unasked: it is sent again only when it provably never reached Parallel, or on a 429 with `Retry-After`. A 408, a 5xx or a connection lost after sending fails `outcome_unknown`, booked at list price, and the run escalates the decision as interrupted (`decision_interrupted`, stage `decision`) for a person. Its key is `PARALLEL_API_KEY`. Its tests run on a fake fetch over 28 recorded responses; the paid REST path is tested only by `test/parallel.live.test.mjs` with a key, and is skipped without one.
- **A decision row can name the admission its request was sent under (`DecisionRow.admission`)**
  - A host that admits each typed-question request before sending it (so a request the run stopped before the answer came back is still counted) records the admission's id on the request's row. The row settles that admission in the row's own commit: a cost fold counts an admission no row names, and never one a row names, so a process stopped between the two counts the request once.
- `DecisionProviderError` takes `unsent: true` from a provider that knows its request was never sent, for example a check before the request that a deadline or a cancel cut. Under a node's `timeoutMs`, `judgeFromProviders` turns a cut `aborted` or `timeout` into `outcome_unknown`, because the request may have been sent and billed, and the decision then goes to review. An `unsent` error keeps its own code instead, so the decision fails as `timeout` (or `aborted`) and asking it again costs nothing. `attempts` alone never says a request was unsent. An error without the field is handled as before.
- Two mechanical predicates for `loop.until`, `escalate.when` and `poll`. `field_type` {path, type} holds when the value at `path` is of a JSON type (`number`, `string`, `boolean`, `array`, `object` or `null`); a number only when finite, and an absent value is of no type. `not` {of} holds when `of` does not, and takes only a mechanical predicate, so a judged `ask` never sits inside a negation. Together they let a guard fail closed on a value that is present but of the wrong type: `escalate` with `{"predicate": "not", "of": {"predicate": "field_type", "path": "score", "type": "number"}}` fires on `"abc"`, `null`, NaN or an object, where `gte` and `lt` on `"abc"` never fire. Validation names a bad `type` or a judged `of` by its path (`when.of`). `FIELD_TYPES` lists the types; the author contract, the workflow schema and the Lean model carry both predicates.
- `GET /v2/capabilities` names each harness's default model: `harnesses[].default_model`, the model this server runs for that harness when a definition names none (`provider/model-id`), or null where it runs none (the harness does not run here, or no default model here runs on it; claude-agent never takes a gateway's model). It is the model a save and a run resolve, through one function (`harnessModel`, which `blockersOf` now calls too), so capabilities and saves cannot disagree. The console reads it: the editor's Model field and the agent page offer each harness the default the server names for it, so a codex agent behind a gateway is offered the gateway's default, and a claude-agent agent is told to choose a model. A server that does not name them keeps the console's earlier rule, pi alone. Capabilities also name the routing prefix this server puts before a gateway's model ids, `models.route_prefix` (`agent-model`; null with no gateway), and the console takes exactly that off a model's label, never a first segment read off the id, so a gateway id with no routing prefix keeps its provider. The exported `capabilities()` helper resolves each harness's offer itself and returns a promise of the document the route serves, `default_model` included, so a direct caller's document passes the published schema. The published document's `Capabilities.models.source` takes on `gateway` and `providers` (marked `models/2`): it held only the contract's `managed`, `chatgpt`, `env` and `faux`, so every answer of a server without `--faux` failed the schema the server publishes.
- **A decision Jev answers is booked at TypeSafe's published price, as an estimate**
  - `jevProvider` prices the counted tokens of a TypeSafe call at `JEV_PUBLISHED_PRICING` ($0.042 per million input tokens, output tokens free) unless `pricing` says otherwise, and books it with `cost_basis: "estimate"`. A cost the response reports still wins, booked `reported`. With no token counts, on the OpenRouter route, or for a call that may have been billed, the cost stays `null`, never 0.
  - agentrun-serve: a decision workflow run's `cost.usd` is its decisions' estimated sum, where it was `null` before.
  - agentrun-serve: a run's usage row keeps a decision's cost to the millionth of a cent, so it agrees with the run's `cost.usd`, and a cost above zero but under a hundredth of a cent reads `<$0.0001`, never `$0.0000`.
- **OpenRouter's Decisions models answer decision nodes, and four-place probabilities are accepted**
  - `openrouterProvider()` in `@parcha/agentrun-decisions` sends a decision to any OpenRouter Decisions model (`POST https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/systemone`) named by the node's `model`, such as `liquid/d1`, `perplexity/pplx-decider-v1.2`, `cloudflare/clef` or `typesafe/jev-1.14`. A node that names no model is refused before sending. The response's `usage.cost` is booked as reported, else the cost is unknown. A request is sent again only when it never reached OpenRouter or on a 429 with `Retry-After`; one lost after sending is `outcome_unknown`. Its key is `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`.
  - Images to OpenRouter (experimental) go in the JSON body as base64 `assets`, only to a model the public catalog lists with image input. Jev takes an image token anywhere in the state or a question, at most 1568 px on a side; every other model only as a whole top-level state value, sent as an item of an array state. A token placed anywhere else is refused before sending.
  - `@parcha/agentrun-decisions/testing/faux-system-one` is a fake System One endpoint for a host's tests and rehearsals.
  - `parallelProvider({ modes })` lists the Parallel modes a node may pin as `parallel-<mode>` (default `PARALLEL_MODES`, `["fast"]`); a node that pins none asks the first.
  - `validateAnswers` accepts probabilities rounded to two, three or four places: a value may be off by half its grid's step. Cloudflare's `clef` reports four places (a distribution summing to 0.9999), which was refused as `probability_mass`.
  - `jevProvider`'s refusal of images on its OpenRouter route names the way that works: the `openrouter` provider with `typesafe/jev-1.14`.
- An agent whose place is `{"mode": "sandbox", "box": "docker"}` runs its `read`, `write`, `edit` and `bash` in a Docker container, on pi, claude-agent and codex alike, while its loop, its log, its model calls and every key stay on the server. The container is made on the run's first such call and labelled with the run's id. It binds the run's `work/` alone at `/run/agentrun/run/work` (a mount source through a link is refused), has no network, gets `LANG` and `HOME` and nothing of the server's environment, and runs as the server's user. It is found again by its label after a crash, the commands the dead process left killed first, and destroyed when the run ends, when it is archived, and by the next start once its run has ended. `bash` takes pi's arguments, with a 300 s default timeout and its output's tail. `read`, `write` and `edit` take a path in the box's work directory only, and their errors name the box's paths, never the server's. This carries the coding-tool source that was proposed on its own (no shell on this machine). `bash` is refused on a runner place, and so is a sandbox place on a workflow, and `browser` in a sandbox place. What the server files in a run's `work/` (monid's long answers, the browser's evidence) is written through held directory handles and never follows a link a box's command planted there. On a host without `/proc/self/fd` (macOS), where a directory is reached by its path, the server files none of these in a sandbox run's `work/`: monid's long answers there say they could not be saved, and the browser's evidence is not filed.
