The agent keeps its memory as files in `work/memory/` (a memory skill: an index, the person's preferences, the accounts it works on); the host seeds the folder into each run and takes it back into the home from the run that owns the sign-in (a plain folder that does not travel in a pack yet), and GET /agent returns the files. With a local Chrome, each run's downloads land in its own `work/downloads/`. The harness entry exports `redactorOf`, `runScrub`, `scanSecretValues`, `scanPlaced` and the `Secret` type, so an example that records a run of its own uses the harness's redaction and scans. `AGENT_MODEL_API=anthropic-messages` runs an Anthropic model over the Messages API at the gateway's root, with pi-ai's own entry, so its thinking is recorded.
- **The chat view puts its dark palette only over an agent's page that declares it supports dark**
  - An agent's page whose own color-scheme names `dark` (a `meta name="color-scheme"`, the root element's style, or a `:root` or `html` rule in its style elements, read with the browser's CSS parser) is drawn in the site's dark palette, as before; the report skill's pages declare `light dark`, so they look as they did.
  - Any other page is drawn as its author wrote it: no forced background, colour, color-scheme or palette variables. A page that sets a light background and leaves its text at the default (a table header `th{background:#eee}`) was white on light grey; it now keeps its own contrast. Square corners and the site's type still go over every page.
- Source-comment corrections in the DSL, no behaviour change: the `effectFiles` JSDoc now names `writeHandoffFiles` (the stale `writeFrozenEvidence` no longer exists), the `executeCode: false` note sits on `validateWorkflow` where it belongs (not on `RETRY_ON_NAMES`), an orphaned `sameValue` comment is dropped, and `applyHostOutputTypes` states why it does not descend into child workflows. The compiled output differs only in these comments.

## 0.1.0-beta.23 (2026-10-22)

- `agentrun.decisions` is now a keyed document family: one document per typed-question receipt, written once, instead of one session document whose `receipts` list was rewritten at every commit.
  - A recording of a run grew with the square of its questions (a 200-item scripted run: 17 MB of decisions in a 22 MB recording); it now grows with the questions (the same run: 0.4 MB of decisions in a 4.9 MB recording; 400 items: 9.8 MB, down from 77 MB).
  - **Breaking for a host that wraps a commit-only session**: `createDecisionWriter`'s session (`DecisionSession.harness`) and `appendDecision`'s first argument now require `commit` and `snapshot` (a `Harness` has both), because the form is the store's and a writer that could not read it would have to guess. A host that passes a real `Harness` changes nothing; one that wraps `{ commit }` alone adds `snapshot` (a TypeScript error today, a refused first row in JavaScript).
  - The row is the same, byte for byte: each keyed document's value is exactly one receipt as before, so `readDecisions`, `appendDecision`, `createDecisionWriter` and `decisionRecovery` keep their behaviour: the same receipts in the same order, and a resumed run answers a question from its receipt without asking again.
  - One form per store: a store that already holds the single document keeps writing it to the end of its run, with no migration, so no recording holds both. A fresh store writes the keyed form, with a small counting document, `agentrun.decisions.count`, beside it.
  - Readers read both forms in the commit before this one, and the harness pins `@parcha/agentrun-pi` exactly, so the two release together: a harness never meets a writer whose rows its cost fold cannot read (a cap that counted from the single document only would let a run spend past it).
- Under the recovery driver, a value an effect adapter returns is the effect's result, whatever its fields. The driver used to read four returned fields as "outcome unknown" (`outcome`, `effect_status`, `details.outcome` or `details.effect_status` equal to `"unknown"`) and stop the run. A tool whose ordinary result had such a field, an inconclusive classifier for example, stopped every run under recovery.

  An adapter that cannot know whether the outside call happened now throws a `RecoveryError` with code `FROZEN_EFFECT_UNKNOWN`, with what it observed as its `cause`. The driver keeps that cause in the checkpoint's `unknownResponses` for the operator. The effect stays unknown and is never dispatched again until the host reconciles it. An `EffectOutcomeUnknownError` works the same way, as any thrown call does.

  Who breaks: a host whose `runEffect` returned one of those fields to mean "unknown". It now throws instead:

      throw new RecoveryError(message, "FROZEN_EFFECT_UNKNOWN", { cause: observed })

  pi does this at its own boundary: a call that `reuseReceipts` refused (`details.effect_status` `"unknown"`) is turned into that throw by `effectResultOf`, new in `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable`. The extension's workflow calls use it.

  A durable task runs the host's own `runEffect`, so a durable host that wraps tools with `reuseReceipts` calls `effectResultOf` on each wrapped tool's result in that `runEffect`. Without it, a refusal is no longer read as unknown and becomes the call's result.
- The recording validator now refuses a live host line that carries an event-level `gen` ("a supervisor line (kind) has no event-level gen", as the viewer says); it accepted one before. A hand-built recording, or one made by a recorder older than this change (a tool box's `box.receipt` written by a process), is refused until the line's `gen` is removed and the process is named in `data.gen`.
- The durable node runner's configuration document, `agentrun.node`, carries the node's input. `value.input` is the node's prompt state: the JSON object its user text was stringified from. A reader can show what a node was given without parsing its prompt.
  - It is optional. It is absent when a node's user text is not a JSON object, and from a document an older build wrote. A run an older build started resumes on that document as it is, and the document is not written again.
  - `NodeDoc`'s value type gains `input?`. Nothing else in the document changes. The attempt's binding digest and the node index do not read the document.
  - Size: on one recorded run of 20 nodes it added about 44 KB, 0.14% of the recording. Each document row grew by 0.5 to 2.2 KB.
- The chat view marks a planned move between hosts. Where the recording holds a host `handoff` line (a run released to its disk on one host and opened on another), the conversation's timeline holds one card there, between the last line of the generation that ended and the first of the next: "after the 5th charge on host-1 (daytona)" over "Moved to host-2 (daytona) from the same disk; the conversation goes on", or, where the holder's recorded label is the one it left, "Reopened on host-1 (local) from the same disk; the conversation goes on". It is read from fields only: the handoff's `point` and `n` (an `effect.kept <tool>` point reads "after the <n>th <tool>", any other point drops that clause), its `from`, and the holder of the generations' `run.json` lines (a host named only by its recorded `host-N` label, a driver only by a kind: local, daytona or modal). The handoff's prose, a process id and a boot id are never read, so none can appear; a `handoff.failed` line draws no card, and a run with no handoff line draws none. A move that happened while a folded conversation was the active one is drawn after that fold, in the run's own timeline. The process lane stays behind `?debug=durability`, and the Muse layout is unchanged.
- The cost fold (`cost`, which `--max-usd` and a demo's spend cap read) and the recording validator read `agentrun.decisions` in both of its forms: the single document (a `receipts` list) and the keyed documents (one row each, `key` its place). A keyed row counts once, however often a snapshot lists it, and a row with no cost makes the cost unknown, never 0.
  - `durableDecisions(file)` (`@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable`) reads a store file's decision rows in commit order in either form; `durableDoc(file, "agentrun.decisions").receipts` is nothing in the keyed form.
  - A recording that holds both forms is refused by the validator: one form per recording.
  - It is the first commit of the change that makes the keyed form, released with it (the harness pins `@parcha/agentrun-pi` exactly).
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/graph` exports `graphDiff(prev, next)`: what changed between two graphs of a workflow, such as two saved revisions of one, as `{ added, removed, changed, moved, edges: { added, removed } }`. A node is the same in both when its `(kind, label)` is unique in each graph, so a step inserted before others reads as one added node and the steps after it moved, never as a run of changes; a node whose `(kind, label)` repeats, or is in one graph only, is matched by its path, and the same path with a new kind or label is changed. A matched node whose kind and label stayed but whose own fields changed (new instructions, a new budget) is rewritten, so a revision that only rewrites steps no longer reads as no change. For that, every node `workflowGraph` returns carries a `changeCheck`: 8 hex digits, the FNV-1a hash of the node's own fields as canonical JSON, its children left out. It detects a change; it is not an identity. Added nodes and edges carry their paths in `next`, removed ones their paths in `prev`. The module still imports nothing.
- `nodeStatus` takes the run's terminal result, `{ pin, items, result }`, and marks where an escalation stopped the run: the escalate node that fired and every container on its path are `escalated`. The driver's frame holds no mark for a fired escalate node, so from the frame alone that node read `not-run` and its containers `working` after the run had ended. A map or loop on that path has ended, so what finished inside it is done. The escalation's node is read from `result.escalation.executionPath`, as `runWorkflow` returns it. `NodeState` widens with `escalated`: a reader that switches on every state adds the case. Without a result, the status is byte for byte what it was.
- A browser run's replay can show what its browser showed: `chat.html?r=<id>&muse&visual` adds a visual report under the answer in the Muse layout. The recording's own browser video leads it (muted, looped, its long holds skipped, scrubbable), with a chapter strip from the pages the run read that seeks it; the run's screenshots follow as a strip of screenshots under each page's title (a blank frame, and a picture shown twice, are no tile); the PDFs an artifact holds are first-page thumbnails; a run that skipped its sign-in because it was remembered says so in a tile; and each fact of the answer is a crop of the frame where it was read, boxed by a look that recorded the box (`manifest.facts`, an `agentrun.facts/1` blob: a frame, a box in the frame's own pixels or none, the look's label and receipt), never by the viewer. At a wide window the report breaks out of the chat column, the video large and the fact cards three across; a phone stays one column. A session that had a credential on its page (`frame.gap`, reason `credential_on_page`) has every frame from its first to the gap's end blurred as pixels, never enlarged, in the strip of screenshots, the crops and the chat's browser card, and the video's playback skips that span; a run with no such gap blurs nothing. Everything is read from the recording's own lines and blobs. Without `&visual` the Muse layout and the default view are as they were.
- `@parcha/pi-agent-learn` is published from this release, under the `beta` tag, with the other packages: its manifest no longer says `"private": true`. It depends on `@parcha/agentrun-dsl`, `@parcha/agentrun-pi` and `@parcha/pi-git-remote` at this release's exact version, and on pi-durable and chord 1.1 to 1.3.x as peers; it needs git 2.40 or later. Its README's "Quickstart (no key, no network)" runs from the packed tarballs in an empty directory with an empty environment in the `stranger` CI job (`scripts/stranger/pi-agent-learn.json`), and fails when its output differs from the README's by a byte. Its README and `packages/pi-agent-learn/CHANGELOG.md` have the details.
- `recordCommits`, `snapshotLines` and `recordingLines` (`@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable`) write a durable `pi.conversation` line for each conversation record (recording draft 54): `data` is `{ id, parent?, owner? }` as pi records them (`parent { conversationId, at }` for a fork, `owner { conversationId, taskId }` for the creator), integer ids and nothing else, placed before its entries in its commit and in the snapshot at open. A bare store read dates a conversation at the first commit that wrote an entry or a document of it, or at the last commit when it has neither. A consumer that counted every line without a `doc` as a task must now skip `kind === "pi.conversation"`.
- `@parcha/pi-git-remote` is published from this release, under the `beta` tag, with the other packages: its manifest no longer says `"private": true`. It depends on none of the other packages and needs git 2.40 or later; `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` stays an optional peer at `*`, for the `./coding-agent` extension only. Its README's "Quickstart (no key, no network)" runs from the packed tarball in an empty directory with an empty environment in the `stranger` CI job (`scripts/stranger/pi-git-remote.json`), and fails when its output differs from the README's by a byte. Its README and `packages/pi-git-remote/CHANGELOG.md` have the details.
- **A recording keeps each conversation's record, and the validator checks it (draft 54)**
  - `apps/viewer/RECORDING.md` adds the `pi.conversation` line, one per conversation record pi writes: `{ id, parent?, owner? }`, with `parent { conversationId, at }` (the conversation it was forked from and the entry it inherits through) and `owner { conversationId, taskId }` (the task that created it), and no content. A panel places a forked conversation under its parent at `parent.at` and ties it to the call that made it through `owner.taskId`. In a conversation with an owner, a `pi.user` entry is its creator's message, never "You", unless a person's submission names it. A copied `reference.label` is never read as a link.
  - `scripts/recording/validate.mjs` refuses a `pi.conversation` that holds anything beyond `id`, `parent` and `owner`, or beyond their two fields each, or whose `parent.conversationId`, `parent.at` (an entry of that conversation) or `owner.taskId` (a task record of `owner.conversationId`, of any kind, found by pi's task record shape) is not in the recording.
- **The chat view draws a conversation a call made under that call, and never draws its maker's words as "You"**
  - A conversation whose record (`pi.conversation`, draft 54) has a `parent` is drawn in its parent conversation at the entry it was forked at (`parent.at`): under the call's card when its owner's task names a tool call, else right after that entry's item, as a fold. A fork is named by its own `reference.label`, else by its parent's label and its place among the parent's conversations (`threads · 1`). It is no longer a top-level "conversation N" row, and a call that made conversations is never folded into the commands line.
  - In a conversation that has an owner, a user entry is its maker's message, drawn with the tool's name (`fork_threads`), never as "You". The one exception is an input a person submitted: a recording that keeps submissions (`manifest.recorded.submissions`, draft 45) names it with a `pi.submission` whose request is not the node runner's.
  - The header names the agent by its own label, never a fork's, even a fork that has no message yet.
  - A jump from the timeline or a citation to anything inside a fork opens every fold around it: the fork's and each conversation's it sits in.
  - A fold inside an open fold keeps its own arrow and preview: the open rule applies to its own summary only.
- `authorWorkflow` no longer evaluates a candidate's code during pre-acceptance validation. Before, when a host allowed executable candidates (`allowExecutableCandidates: true`), the structural validation of each candidate compiled and ran its `code` nodes against probe states in the host process, so an author-written candidate's code ran before the host's acceptance hook decided on it. Validation now checks a candidate's code for syntax only; the code runs only through a later run or dry run, after the host accepts the candidate. Hosts that leave executable candidates off (the default) were never affected. The documented note stands: a `code` node runs in the host process and is not a sandbox, so hosts isolate untrusted code themselves.
- A recovery driver waits out an effect's `deadline_s` and a poll's `interval_s` whatever their size. Before, a deadline with a fraction of a millisecond (`deadline_s: 1.0005`) threw `ERR_OUT_OF_RANGE` once the effect was admitted, so the effect was never dispatched and was left unknown. A deadline past Node's timer limit of 2^31-1 ms, about 24.8 days (`deadline_s: 3000000`), fired at once with a `TimeoutOverflowWarning`, aborting the effect as it started and leaving it unknown. A poll wait past that limit checked again with no wait at all. The effect's deadline still aborts with the `TimeoutError` a host reads as a deadline.
- Validation refuses a declared write to any state key that holds a `$`, not only one that starts with it. A typed question's sidecars, `<as>$answers` and `<as>$verify`, were documented as engine-owned, yet a node could write `gate$answers` by `as`, by an unaliased label, or by a collecting map's `failedAs`, and overwrite the answers a code gate reads. The label message now reads `label must not contain "$"`, and the author contract says keys that hold a `$` are engine-owned. Reading a sidecar is unchanged. A code node's patch may still write a `$` key at run time, as the Lean model documents (finding F3).
- `inspectWorkflow` and the shape diagnostics name what a judged route lacks. The workflow schema has two route variants, a judged route (`instructions`, `state`) and a route by value (`valuePath`), and the diagnostics kept only the last schema per node kind. So a judged route missing its `state` was told it must have a `valuePath`. Every variant of a kind is now kept, and a node is diagnosed against the variant that accepts it, or else the one it is closest to. What the full validator accepts is unchanged.
- A recovery driver whose pause or cancel commit the store refuses now says so: `flush()` throws the refused commit, and `close()` throws it after releasing the journal. Before, both resolved, the journal still read `running`, and the next open resumed a run the host had been told was cancelled. The run reports the stop (`FROZEN_CANCELLED` or `FROZEN_PAUSED`), or the refusal itself when the refusal reaches one of the run's own commit waits first; a host that awaits `close()` and sees it throw must not take the run as stopped, and can open it and stop it again. A run's own failed commit is unchanged: the run and `flush()` throw it, and `close()` does not throw it again. A durable pi run whose journal refuses a cancel taken when its next process starts hands its waiters that refusal, where they got a poisoned-Session error before; the task stays pending, and a later process takes the cancel.
- Errors from `createJevRunner` now say what happened, and its checks match what was sent. A host that branches on `code` or `requestDiagnostic.reason` gets the right branch.
  - A deadline that the SDK's own per-attempt timer reaches first is `timeout`, and is not retried, as when the runner's own deadline ends a call. When the SDK's timer ended the last attempt, it was `connection`: in 300 runs of a request that never answered, with one attempt, 8 to 18 came back `connection`. With attempts left, the runner's deadline ended the retry wait, so the call was `timeout` and no second request went out. A timed-out request may have been billed, and the code is what tells a host so. With `timeoutMs: null` there is no deadline: a host client's own timeout stays a connection failure and is retried.
  - A `null` or missing `state` is refused before sending, as `invalid_request` with `attempts` 0 and `requestDiagnostic.reason` `invalid_state`. Before, it passed the runner's own check and the SDK refused it after the attempt began: `invalid_request` with `attempts` 1 and no diagnostic. A custom client that took a `null` state is refused it too.
  - `client: null` from a JavaScript caller is no client. Before, it lifted the rule that the SDK path needs a finite `timeoutMs`, and let an image or a Box question go to OpenRouter.
  - A blank per-call `model` is refused with `requestDiagnostic.reason` `unsupported_model`, the reason a model refusal already had. It was `invalid_questions`, which named the question map.
  - Answers are checked against the questions as they were sent. A criterion whose value is `undefined` is not carried by JSON, so Jev is never asked about it; it is no longer required in the answer, and a right answer to the questions sent is no longer refused as `invalid_response`. The DSL's interpreter still checks a workflow's answers against its own questions.
  - An `apiKey` that is empty or only spaces is a configuration error when the runner is created, on the TypeSafe path as it already was on OpenRouter. Before, it was sent as an empty bearer token and every call failed as `http` 401.
  - `@parcha/agentrun-pi`'s message for an invalid Jev state no longer lists `null` as accepted.
- `createWorkflowTool` from `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable` now checks its host's `tools` as the coding-agent extension checks `hostTools`, and throws when it is created with a list that fails: more than 100 tools, a name that is blank, longer than 200 characters, repeated, or reserved (the workflow tool's own name, `agentrun` by default, and the node runner's `submit`), or `parameters` that are not an object schema. Before, such a list was taken as given. Both hosts use one check.
- A fork of a workflow node's conversation no longer hangs. The node's configuration document (`agentrun.node`) was forked with the conversation, so the fork's first request waited for the node runner, which never reaches a fork. A fork now starts without it, as it already did without the node's record: it is no node, it answers on its own, and a `submit` in it is refused as in any conversation that is no workflow node. A host that reset the document in the fork by hand can drop that step.
- A limit given as `undefined` now keeps its default, as an omitted one does, in `createAgentRunExtension`'s `runtimeLimits` and in `WorkflowExtensionService`'s `limits`. Before, a field present with the value `undefined` (as an options object built from optional configuration has) was refused as an invalid limit. An explicit `null` still lifts a limit, and any other invalid value is still refused.
- A recording opened from a dropped folder (or the folder picker) now reads each blob as the gallery publishes it: an extension outside the recording format's allowlist (draft 50), such as an `.svg`, a page or a script, is text, and its `blob:` URL is `text/plain`. Before, a dropped `<sha>.svg` kept the browser's own `image/svg+xml`, so its `blob:` URL, which has the viewer's origin, would run a script it carried if it was opened in a tab. Every dropped blob's URL now carries the viewer's type, never the file's own, and a type that would run in a tab is opaque bytes, as before. A dropped artifact page (`.html`) is read as text, as a files build already reads it.
- The pi-transport test that checks a checkpoint counts its held time from when the session came to rest, not from the pull's ask, no longer fails on a loaded runner: it compares the held time with the measured total (held is at most the total minus the 400 ms drain) instead of an absolute 300 ms bound.
- The release workflow publishes each package with npm's `latest` dist-tag, so npm's package pages show the README. npm takes a package page's README from the publish that carries `latest`; publishing with `--tag beta` and moving `latest` later with `npm dist-tag add` left every `@parcha` page without one. A release now moves `latest` when it publishes, and a maintainer moves `beta` to the same version afterwards.
- The `stranger` CI job runs the "Quickstart (no key, no network)" of `@parcha/pi-git-remote` and of `@parcha/pi-agent-learn` from their packed tarballs in an empty directory, with an empty environment and home (`scripts/stranger/pi-git-remote.json`, `scripts/stranger/pi-agent-learn.json`). pi-agent-learn's spec packs the packages it depends on too (`dsl`, `jev`, `pi` and `pi-git-remote`) and installs pi-durable, chord and pi-ai beside it. Each spec requires the output its README shows under the quickstart, anchored at both ends, and the learn spec reads `<sha>` as the 12 hex digits of the seeded commit. A test in `scripts/stranger.test.mjs` holds each pattern to its README block, so the two cannot drift.
- The README no longer says every node shows on a graph in a viewer, which the public viewer does not draw today. It says that `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/graph` turns a workflow into its graph. The issue-triage example's README and author prompt drop the same claim. Pi's `/agentrun` graph is unchanged and still documented.
- The README has a "Durable runs" section (`openRecovery`, `fileStore`, `withRecovery`: a run opened again with the same key answers committed steps from the journal and runs the rest), with a sample that dies and resumes with no key, and an "A judge on any model" sample whose `runJudge` is a plain function. It no longer says that hosts own recovery: the host chooses the store and the package ships the driver.
- The README pins every peer install line to `@1.3.0` (typebox to `1.3.7`) and says that Pi 1.4 is not supported yet: an unpinned install now resolves Pi 1.4.0, and an unpinned pi-durable or chord fails with `ERESOLVE` against the peer range. It no longer says a run "is not checkpoint resume" next to the paragraph that says an interrupted run continues, and it has a new section on running a workflow as a durable pi-durable task (`createWorkflowTool`, `createWorkflowExtension`): what a resume answers from the journal and what it refuses.
- The recovery models state the host's cancel. `spec/receipts/Receipts.tla` adds a stop whose commit may fail and the host letting go of the driver with `close`, with three properties: a stop the host let go of is on the journal or the host heard that it is not (`I8_StopOnJournalOrHeard`), nothing is admitted or dispatched after a stop (`I9_NoAdmissionAfterStop`), and a cancelled run never resumes (`I10_CancelledNeverResumes`), each with a guard mutant TLC must catch. `spec/lean/AgentRunRecovery/Frozen.lean` proves the same for every number of steps and crashes (`stop_admits_nothing`, `stop_dispatches_nothing`, `cancelled_never_resumes`), with a witness that a refused stop commit is reached and heard.

## 0.1.0-beta.21 (2026-10-21)

- A gallery lock entry (`apps/viewer/recordings.lock.json`) may now name the words its list shows for a run (`title`: a trimmed string of 1 to 120 characters, no control character) and the view its link opens (`view`: a non-empty list of distinct flags the viewer knows, today `muse`, the phone-chat layout). The recordings are not touched: the lock is read by the build, which carries each named id's `title` and `view` into its gallery entry (`gallery.js`, or `recordings/index.json` in a files build), the list shows `title` in place of the recording's own words and links `chat.html?r=<id>` with each flag (`&muse`), and an entry without them is listed and linked exactly as before. `readLock` refuses a wrong type, an empty or untrimmed or over-long title, an unknown or repeated flag, an empty list, and any other key; the gate refuses a built index whose titles or views differ from the lock's. The three utility-account replays in the lock open in the Muse layout under their own titles. The inspection header and the default chat header still use the recording's own title.
- Validation with input keys now refuses a state read that a run would refuse, inside a route's branches and past route and parallel nodes. Before, the state check stood down there: a `{url}` that nothing produced passed `validateWorkflow` (and so inspection) and failed the run with `interpolation path "url" is not present in the state`, while the same read at the top level was refused. The check now holds what each point of the run holds:
  - inside a route branch, the state before the route plus the route's `as` (and `<as>$answers` for a route Jev chooses);
  - after a route, only the keys every branch writes, since one of them runs;
  - after a parallel, what each branch writes, since all of them run.

  It still stands down after a code node, a map or a loop, as before.

  A workflow that read, after a route, a key only some branches write is now refused at validation, where before it failed at run time through a branch that does not write it. Write the key on every branch, or read it inside the branch that writes it.
- The gallery's list now follows the lock's order. `build()` lists the recordings in the order of the lock's ids when it is given a lock (the ids the lock does not name come after, by name), the gate checks that the built index lists them in that order, and the lock's ids are in the order the gallery should show: the three earlier recordings as they stand today, then the three utility-account replays as a story told in parts. A lock entry's `title` also refuses the C1 control characters (U+0080 to U+009F), as it already refused the C0 ones and DEL. The recordings and the asset are untouched; the tar, written with its members sorted by path and read by id, does not depend on the lock's order.
- **`examples/issue-triage`: the host counts the agreement whatever shape the agent's workflow returns**
  - The session counted the type agreement only when the workflow's result was an object with `rows`; a workflow that returned the list itself got no agreement and a result blob with numbered keys. The rows are now the result itself when it is a list, else its `rows`, and the recording's result holds `{ rows, agreement }` either way.
  - The summary line reads the spend before the recording is finished (the spool is removed then), so `spentUsd` is the run's spend and not 0.
- The DSL package's build and type-check reject an unused local, import or parameter (`noUnusedLocals`, `noUnusedParameters`), and the two unused imports they found in the recovery handoff block are gone. The emitted JavaScript and declarations are byte for byte what they were.
- The README now shows a `runWorkflow` call a reader can run with no key (a scripted `runNode` answers each model step), a CLI `run` line that works, how to import `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/schema` (as JSON), and a link to the host integration guide.
- The README now starts with the offline sample, which needs no key, then the live call. It says that a missing key fails at `createJevRunner`, before any request, that the TypeScript samples need a TypeScript runner, and it lists the whole public API. It also describes the `Retry-After` wait and the 429 give-up, the `requestDiagnostic` on a refused request, and that `cost_usd` is the reported `usage.cost` first.
- The README now says that `pi --offline` needs an interactive terminal, that its two startup warnings (`fd not found`, `No models available`) are expected, and what the trust prompt offers. It marks the preview, input, run, save and load steps as needing a model, links the example that lives in the repository, adds `/agentrun triage`, labels the host samples as sketches, and documents the `agentrun-pi` command line from npm with an inputs example.

## 0.1.0-beta.20 (2026-10-20)

- **The gallery takes a recording that names no example under an id of its own, and shows three utility-account browser replays**
  - A recording whose manifest names an example (its `example`, else a `task` that is an example folder's name) is still that example's: its id is the folder's name, one recording per example, so a re-take replaces it. A recording that names none (its `example` absent or null, its task free text, as in a browser session's replay) may take any id of the lock's form except an example folder's name, so an example's id only ever holds that example's own run. `scripts/recording/gallery.mjs` refuses `<id>: is the example folder examples/<id>, but its manifest names no example`; without the folder list, the check is as before.
  - The gallery's lock names a new asset, `recordings-6b.tar`: the three recordings it held, byte for byte, and three replays of a browser agent at a utility account (`utility-bills`, `utility-amount-owed`, `utility-last-payment`).
- A recording can be opened as a phone chat of the agent's work: `chat.html?r=<id>&muse` is a different layout over the same events, in the viewer's own agentrun.ai look (its tokens, square corners, hairlines, flat cards, JetBrains Mono for labels and titles, orange the one accent), and without `&muse` a recording reads as before (a test pins the default view byte for byte). A square avatar at the top, one picture file the build ships beside the page (`muse-avatar.webp`), floats over the conversation with the agent's name (the host's `reference.label`, else the recording's title) and its state: "Waiting for you" when a hold is open, "Done" once the run delivered and the end of the conversation has been scrolled into view, else "is working". The header and the composer are transparent: the chat scrolls under them behind a soft blur and fade. The person's words are a hairline box under a "You" label, the agent's replies sit under its name with the files it names as chips, and its thinking and the steps between are folded and quiet. The browser is a card inline in the chat: the frame the steps ended on and a status line, "Your agent is driving the browser" (no button: the shipped browser viewer has none); a hold, read from the `browser.sessions` and `browser.handoff` rows alone, reads "Handed the browser over: <what was asked>" and then "Handed back · <outcome>". Where the run waited for a person to sign in, a "Secure Store" card marks it, from the browser's own `browser.profiles` document (the address it gains, then its verification; "Signed in · remembered" when it was already there). The host's artifact is a "What I remember" card (times as a fixed date, no file names or web addresses), read by decoding the recording's embedded `data:` page in the viewer itself, so it reads under a host whose policy allows `connect-src 'self'` only. No report page beside it, and every web address is shortened to its host.

## 0.1.0-beta.19 (2026-10-20)

- **`examples/issue-triage`: an agent writes the workflow that triages a repository's issues with Jev**
  - `session.ts` gives a lead model the `agentrun` tool and one sentence: it drafts a typed workflow, checks it (`inspect`), dry-runs it for free and runs it as one durable task, over a repository's newest labelled issues. For each issue a research agent with read-only tools reads the issue, the pull requests that mention it and the code it names; Jev gives it one of four types and judges good-first-issue and actionable in one request; the result is a table of rows, and the host counts how often Jev's type agrees with the maintainers' labels, which the classifier never sees. `--record` keeps the run as a recording whose graph the viewer replays. `--max-usd` is a hard spend stop, and the host (not the agent) counts how often the type matches the maintainers' labels.
  - The run needs no GitHub token: two API requests per issue, the rest from `github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` at the one commit the run was picked at (`pick-issues.mjs` prints it with the issues). A rate limit stops the run with that reason and is not retried.
  - `npm run demo:issue-triage` runs the same workflow on scripted fakes ($0, no key, no network) over twenty fictional issues, through the harness's demo entry (`--record`). `live.ts` takes the lead and Jev from your keys or from an OpenAI-compatible gateway (`/typesafe` for Jev). `workflow.json` is the reference workflow (it validates and dry-runs clean); `AUTHOR-PROMPT.md` is what a user says to the drafting agent; `SETUP-PROMPT.md` and `demo.json` are the setup prompt and its spec, held to each other by `scripts/check-setup-prompts.mjs`. Recovery from a killed process is tested (`--kill`, `analyze.mjs`), under "For developers" in the README.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/graph` exports `graphNodeOf(graph, executionPath)`: the graph node an execution path ran at, `{ path, at }`, with `at` the map items and loop iterations it ran in, outermost first (`{ kind: "item" | "iteration", index }`). An execution path is the node's JSON Pointer with `items/<i>` before a map's `body` and `iterations/<i>` before a loop's; `graphNodeOf` reads it by walking the graph by node kind, so a route branch named `items` or `iterations` stays a branch, and a path the graph does not have, or one that is not an execution path, is `undefined`. The module still imports nothing.
- A map can collect its items' failures: `onItemFailure: "collect"` with `failedAs: "<key>"`. An item that ends on an item failure becomes a row `{ item_index, label, code, message }` of `failedAs`, in item order, the other items finish, and `as` stays aligned with the items: `as[i]` is item i's result, or `null` when item i failed. The default, `onItemFailure: "stop"`, is the behaviour before: the first failing item stops the map and the run. The map never escalates by itself; gate on `failedAs` with `escalate` or a code node.

  - An item failure is one of the interpreter's own typed node failures (an `EffectFailure` after its retries, `WorkflowOutputInvalidError`, `WorkflowStateError`, `WorkflowCodeError`, `WorkflowVerificationError`) or a host error that declares itself one, with a string `code` and `itemFailure: true`. `isItemFailure(error)` is the rule, exported from `@parcha/agentrun-dsl`. Never collected: an effect whose outcome is unknown, any `RecoveryError`, an `escalate` inside the item, a stop or cancel, several failures at once, and anything else. Each collected row emits `map.item_failed` `{ item_index, label, code }`. When several branches of a parallel (or items of a map that stops) fail at once, the failure reported is the first one, unless that is an item failure and another is not: then it is the other, so an item failure never hides a failure a collecting map must stop on. A row's `label` is the node that failed: the innermost step, or a structural node (a nested map whose list is not one) when the failure is its own.
  - The validator: `onItemFailure` is `stop` or `collect`; `failedAs` is required with `collect`, refused without it, may not begin with `$` and differs from `as`; parallel branches may not both write it. Each refusal is an issue at the map's pointer: a bad `onItemFailure`, a missing `failedAs` or one equal to `as` `NODE_FIELD_INVALID`, a `$` `failedAs` `STATE_KEY_RESERVED`, `failedAs` without `collect` `NODE_KEY_UNKNOWN` (a key of the other form), two branches writing it `PARALLEL_WRITE_CONFLICT`. The schema, the author contract and the guide say so.
  - Recovery: `WorkflowDeps.recovery` gains `failItem` and `itemFailure`. A collected failure commits with its item before the map goes on, and every later process ends the item on it without running its body. A store without both hooks cannot hold a collecting map: it is refused before any step runs. The recovery driver keeps the row in its frame's `failures` (the whole layout) or the item's `agentrun.items` record (`status: "failed"`, `outcome`, in the keyed layout), and a step that failed on an item failure keeps it (`failure`), so a process that resumes between the step's commit and the item's ends the item the same way. An item whose call it left with an unknown outcome is refused as `FROZEN_EFFECT_UNKNOWN`, never collected.
  - `@parcha/agentrun-pi`: `NodeFailure.itemFailure` is true when the node's delivery attempts are spent or its model will not answer (`model_error`, as a provider's content filter), never for an abort, a stale or faulted run, a missing model or a schema no record can satisfy.
  - Deploy rule: run a collecting workflow only once every process that can open the run is on this release or later. A process on an earlier release refuses the workflow at validation, so its open fails its task, and that ends every agent node of the run then in flight. With the node runner's new attempt for a replaced task (which this release needs under it) those nodes start again from their beginning in new conversations, so the run still completes, but each such open costs those agent turns again: in the reference kill storm (30 kills a seed), 4.27 to 4.30 model requests per committed turn with old processes against 3.04 and 3.40 without (the medians of four 50-seed storms), about 33% more.
- A workflow node that names no `thinking` level thinks at medium (it was low), on every Pi path: the runner, `/agentrun` and the durable node runner. Thinking is never off: a level of `off` from any source runs at medium. A node that names medium or high costs the same as before. A node that names no level and ran at low now costs more per request, and so does one that ran at off.
  - `createPiRunner`: a node's own `thinking`, else the runner's `thinkingLevel` option (the caller's override), else medium.
  - `createPiHostRunner` and `/agentrun`: the host session's thinking level is no longer a node's default. A node that names none thinks at medium whatever level the session runs at, `off` included. Pass `thinkingLevel` to `createPiHostRunner` to choose another default.
  - The durable node runner (`@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable`): a node's own `thinking`, else the level the host's `agent(node)` sets, else medium. Before, the runner left the level to the host's agent alone, so a node's own `thinking` counted only when the host applied it. A node a task owns copied its owner conversation's level, and a node no task owns ran with thinking off. A node conversation the runner created before this change keeps its level when it resumes; one an older build opened, which the runner gives its agent when it reaches the node, takes the new level.
  - The author reference (`language.md`) states the default: a node that names no `thinking` thinks at medium, unless its host sets another level.
- A rejected workflow says where and which rule: `validateWorkflow` returns `issues` beside `errors`, one `{ path, code, message }` per error in the same order, with `message` the same text, and `WorkflowInvalidError` carries the same `issues`. `errors` is unchanged. `path` is a JSON Pointer into the workflow as written naming what the rule is about (a node, at the same path `inspectWorkflow` gives it; a schema; or `""` for the document's own fields and a supplied input), never a missing key. `code` is one of 16 rules, the exported `ValidationCode`; the guide lists each with an example. The set may grow, so keep a default branch.

  A dry run says whether every check ran: `dryRunWorkflow` results gain `status`, `passed`, `not_run` (`ok` stays true and `skipped` gives the reasons, as before) or `failed`. `ok` and the CLI's exit codes are unchanged; `agentrun dry-run --strict` exits 3 when the status is `not_run`.
- The chat view reads as a conversation: a run of steps that only ran a command folds into one quiet "ran N commands" line, and a step that made looks leads with them (each look's picture, what it asked, and the first sentence of its answer that says something), with the code behind a click.
  The person's question is a plain bubble with the files as chips (four, then "+ N files") and any other fields behind "details"; the first screenful of pictures loads at once.
  The chat view and the inspection view take agentrun.ai's look: square corners, no accent bars, the site's dark palette and its type. The agent's page is drawn in that look too, for presentation only: the recording keeps its own page byte for byte.
  A thought shows a title only when its model wrote one (a headline in bold); any other thought folds whole under "Thought". A look's card shows its first picture that is not near-black, so a film's dark opening frame never leads.
- **`apps/viewer`: the hosted gallery's recordings come from a release asset, and a public replay draws no dollar figure**
  - The gallery's recordings leave git. `apps/viewer/recordings.lock.json` names one tar on the release tagged `gallery`,
    its SHA-256 and the gallery's ids in order, each `{ id, poster?, looks? }` (`looks`: the keys of looks the run filed on
    one image, which its card shows with their boxes). The Pages job fetches it before the build
    (`node scripts/recording/gallery.mjs fetch`, with the job's own token) and unpacks nothing unless the tar hashes to
    the lock, holds only those ids' manifests, lines and blobs as plain files, no recording is over 80 MB and the gallery
    not over 300 MB, raw, each poster is an image its recording holds, and each id, listed once, is the name of the example
    folder whose run it is (`examples/<id>`), as the run's own manifest says: one recording per example, so a re-take
    replaces its id. `gallery.mjs pack` writes the tar and the lock's SHA-256.
  - The gallery's gate reads a card on an index entry (`{ title, line?, poster?, rank? }`) and refuses a poster that is
    not an image the recording holds.
  - A part of the inspection view that draws a dollar figure is marked `cost` and drawn only with `?debug=cost` or
    `?debug=durability`, under Receipts too: the strip's cost, the vision panel's spend and a receipt's price, and the
    transport panel's cost.
  - The vision panel no longer says the eyes have not looked when live lines tell of a look no store document filed: it
    lists the look (a native one as the lead's own) under what live lines told, as never filed once the run delivered,
    and counts nothing from it.
  - The gallery shows agent runs only: the scripted reference job leaves `apps/viewer/recordings/` for the test fixtures
    (`apps/viewer/test/fixtures/reference-job`), where every test that read it now finds it. In git, `recordings/` holds
    only its README.
  - A page, an SVG or a script in a recording (an agent's report, draft 41) is published as `<name>.txt`: opened straight on
    the gallery's site it is plain text and runs nothing, while the viewer reads it into its sandboxed frame. The gate
    refuses a published blob that would open as a page under its own name.
  - The viewer's page carries a Content-Security-Policy (only its own files run or load; data: and blob: pictures and
    videos), its styles in `viewer.css`; PDF.js opens every PDF with `isEvalSupported: false`.
- The release workflow's publish job may run 90 minutes (it was 45): enough for its `npm run check` (about 27 minutes) and one registry wait per package (up to 20 minutes each).
- `docs/guide.md` has a section "An agent that learns", after the Pi workflow section: notes proposed on the run's own branch and curated into commits on `main`, a bad lesson reverted or retired, and saved workflows judged on their own document, admitted as trial skills, promoted on clean uses and demoted on escalations. Its part "Its own git repository" has a short program for the repository the agent keeps. `docs/examples.md` lists `examples/agent-learn` (`npm run demo:learn`).
- The `@parcha/agentrun-dsl` README and the guide's "Let Pi build and run a workflow" tell the story in five steps: the agent drafts a typed workflow, checks it, dry-runs it for free, runs it, and every node shows on its graph. Each step names the call or the `/agentrun` command that does it.
  Install and `npx` lines use the `beta` dist-tag (`npx @parcha/agentrun-dsl@beta`, since npm has no unscoped `agentrun`), the starter's README names no version (it installs at the version its `package.json` pins), and the READMEs state one Pi range (1.0.4 to below 1.4, with the versions CI tests).
  The `@parcha/agentrun-dsl` README says that a durable run's store keeps each effect's arguments as given, so secrets are redacted before they reach an effect.
  The author skill and the guide say that a dry run gives a boolean whose schema leaves it open (no `const` or `enum`) the value `false`, so an `escalate` on such a boolean being `false` always fires there and the dry run reports `not_run`.
  The Jev README, its types and its Box refusal no longer say that `jev-latest` is 1.13: a Box question needs `jev-1.14.0` (or later) or `jev-preview` by name, and the runner refuses it on `jev-latest` whatever version that alias points at.

## 0.1.0-beta.18 (2026-10-19)

- The gallery opens a recorded run in a chat + report view (`apps/viewer/chat.html?r=<id>`): the run's recorded conversations on the left, the agent's report on the right. The inspection replay is one link away, "How this ran" (`./?r=<id>`).
  The left side is made from the recording's generic events, the same for every run: the run's input as given as the user's message, then each conversation as a thread (its messages as recorded without the prompt the host composed to open it, the assistant's text as bubbles between the cards, the run's own thinking folded, one card per tool call titled by the tool and its arguments, consecutive calls of one tool showing the first three, and under each tool card every model call it made as its own card with its scores, the boxes it drew and its raw output, a judge's calls marked with the judge's accent), and a session timeline of workflow nodes, LLM calls, tool calls and the model calls a tool makes, nested under it and labelled as their cards.
  The right side is the agent's own artifact when the recording carries one (`manifest.artifact`), shown in a frame that runs no script, its citations opening the cited page or frame with its region boxed; otherwise the run's cited answer, or the result record it submitted, labelled as the answer; otherwise its final assistant text.
  The view shows no counters, costs, process or kill markers, and no thinking the run did not record.
- The Jev runner honours `Retry-After` on HTTP 429 and 503. It waits the larger of the server's time and its own backoff, never past the request's timeout or its abort signal, and gives up at once when the server asks for longer than is left. A request still rate-limited after its retries ends as before: `JevError` with code `http` and status 429.
- **`apps/viewer`: the gallery and a replay's header name no process or kill**
  - The killed and resumed reference job (`ten-kills-seed21`) leaves the gallery and is kept with the test fixtures
    (`apps/viewer/test/fixtures/`), where the tests that read a killed run beside the clean one find it.
  - The gallery's line reads "Replays a run recorded as agentrun.recording/1.", each run in its list is followed by its
    model alone, and a replay's header no longer counts processes and kills. The counters stay under `?debug=durability`.
- The vision agent example (`examples/vision-agent`) with a gateway set (`VIDEO_MODEL_BASE_URL`) now reaches an Anthropic lead or eyes model over Anthropic's own Messages API at the gateway's root (`/v1/messages`), under the gateway's key, instead of over chat completions. Through chat completions the gateway answered with thinking off (0 reasoning tokens, no reasoning field); over the Messages API the agent node's `thinking` level (medium) is Anthropic's adaptive thinking at that effort, and the model's thinking comes back as thinking blocks when it thinks (adaptive thinking is the model's choice at that effort: on a live probe Sonnet 5.5 returned a thinking block at `high` and none at `medium`). A gateway that serves an Anthropic model only on its OpenAI-compatible endpoints must also serve `/v1/messages` for it. `--thinking low|medium|high` sets the agent node's thinking for a run (the default stays `medium`; any other value, or an input it cannot be put in (a file, a capture name, none), is refused before anything starts), which is how a Claude lead is asked to think at `high`. OpenAI and Google models, and runs on a provider's own key, are unchanged.
- The vision agent example (`examples/vision-agent`) finds its example folder as the one argument that is neither a flag nor the value of a flag that takes one, so `npm run demo:vision -- --live examples/document-check` asks that folder's question over its files instead of silently running the default question over none. The flags that take a value are the host's string options and `--thinking`; a test holds the list to the host's. A second folder, a stray token, or a folder that is not an example is refused before anything starts, with one line and nothing written, where it used to be skipped or to end in a stack trace.
- On fakes, the vision agent's scripted lead looks at a first file that is a still image (PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF) as it is, and cites the image itself. Before, it read any file that was not a PDF as a film and asked for its frame at second 1, which a still image does not have, so a free `examples/lake-mead` run stopped with "the model did not answer". PDFs and films are read as before.
- The vision agent example (`examples/vision-agent`) with a gateway set (`VIDEO_MODEL_BASE_URL`) no longer asks for `TYPESAFE_API_KEY`: Jev goes through the gateway's `/typesafe`, so its key is `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` when given and the gateway's `VIDEO_MODEL_API_KEY` otherwise; without a gateway `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is still required. A live run now checks every key before it starts anything, naming each one that is missing and what it is for (`--live is missing TYPESAFE_API_KEY (typesafe.ai: ...); OPENAI_API_KEY (the lead openai/gpt-6-luna)`), and stops with a failing code, a message and nothing written: no recording, no run directory and no `cost: unknown` line, where it used to start the recorder first and leave a recording of the refusal behind. `demo.json`, the README and the setup prompt say the same. The scripted lead of a run on fakes now writes its report as `report/index.html`, the name the report skill gives a live run's, and the instructions of a package without the skill name it too.
- The vision agent example reads the report page its answer names only from under `work/report/`. A path that leaves `report/`, by its parts or through a link (`report/` itself, a folder in it, or the page), gives the record it gives for a missing page, with a note on the answer's row that says why, and nothing it names is read or kept. A file in `report/` that is a link is not kept with the report. The Answer schema refuses a report path with a `.` or `..` part.
- Two more questions for the vision agent, each a folder with no code: `examples/lake-mead` (thirteen Landsat images of Lake Mead, 1984 to 2024, committed; when the water fell most and every shoreline it left, boxed and dated) and `examples/market-street` (the 1906 film down Market Street from the Library of Congress, fetched by URL; every vehicle that crosses in front of the camera, to the second, boxed and counted). `ASSETS.md` records each source and its licence as the source page states it.

## 0.1.0-beta.17 (2026-10-18)

- The hero demo moves onto the showcase template: `demo.json` (title, targets, claim, models, keys, kills, panels, tools, expected cost), `job.ts` as data (a workflow and its default input, which now names the operators to read), `fakes.ts`, a short `run.ts` on the reference entry, `SETUP-PROMPT.md` and a README in the template's shape. `npm run demo:hero` takes the standard flags; `--kill` replaces `--kill1`, and `--input` takes a subset of operators. The hero's own looks filing is gone: the entry's vision option keeps every look. The reference entry gains `Fakes.backend` (a demo shapes what its fake browser serves).
- The reference entry takes `--provider`, `--model` and `--eyes-model` (with `--live`; handed to the demo's `live` as `models`), `--see-also <recording>` (linked in the manifest's `see_also` by name and title), and a demo's `manifest(context)` hook for its own manifest fields (a name the entry writes is refused). `source.run` is now the demo's name plus the input file's name, not a fixed `reference`.
- The reference example's child is also an app module for `pi-durable-disk run --app` (`examples/reference/child-app.ts`), so a run has one open path with the disk package's claim, fence, stdin mount token, run.json holder and restarts. On `done` the run's final status carries the record and the store's hash. `run()` is now built from `compose()` (the registry, models and the steps after the open) and the entry's child from `prepareChild()`, which the app module shares.
  A run is keyed by its demo's name (the place's `paths.id`, passed to the child as `RUN_ID`), not a fixed `reference`, so two demos never share a run; `pathsAt` and `placeAt` take the id. Under `pi-durable-disk run --app` the id is the instance's `--id`: pass the demo's name.
  The supervisor puts `DEMO_ENTRY` (the demo's entry module) and `DEMO_NAME` into every order's env, so a place that starts the child through the app module needs no wrapper.
  A process run by `pi-durable-disk run --app` writes nothing under the run's directory before its claim is open: the recorder, the fetched sources, the box, the ledger and event logs and the judge's count start in `beforeResume`, and a recording line produced earlier is kept in memory and written in order when the recorder starts.
- The reference entry has an opt-in code mode (`DemoOptions.codeMode`, or `codeMode` in demo.json): a `code` tool whose script runs in pi-codemode's sandbox with `tools.bash` (through the call's environment, in `work/`) and the vision handle's `vision.see`. Off by default; the sandbox has no network, process or file access of its own, and a command it runs can do what the environment's shell can.
  The repository now declares `@earendil-works/pi-codemode` as a root devDependency, the one extra install code mode needs.
- The supervisor writes `delegation` host lines from `place.status()`'s `delegations` when one appears or its orphaned or pending state changes, and a `revoke` line from `recover()`'s `{ revoked, ms }`; a place without them writes none.
  The supervisor writes a `box.receipt` host line for each box `place.status().boxes` reports once its `to` appears (or at the final read): the box as a `box-N` label, seconds, the published per-second price and the cost (null with no price), the source and whether it was priced; never the provider's id. A place that reports none writes none.
  The example's cost (`examples/reference/fold.ts`) counts each `box.receipt`'s `cost_usd` once per box label; a receipt with none (an unpriced box, or one still open at the final read) makes the cost unknown, never 0.
- The reference entry takes `Fakes.input` (the no-key default input, a smaller slice than the job's own; `--input` wins), reports the models the live parts chose (`LiveParts.models`, handed to `manifest()` as `models`, so a demo needs no file handshake between its two hooks), and prints why a child stopped in the summary.
  A demo's vision lane, `fakes.vision(host, running, { input })` and the live parts' `vision(host, running, { input })`, is handed the input the run was given (`--input`, else the mode's default), so scripted eyes need not read the entry's environment.
  The same third argument carries the run's `place` (`{ root, work }`, as a tool's context has it).
- `models.judge.caps` in demo.json (`{ requests, tokens }`, each a positive integer at most 4 times the default of 50 and 40,000) sets the demo's own Jev caps; a value past the ceiling is refused at load, the run still refuses past the caps and `--max-usd` still applies. A demo that names `models.judge` (Jev) without a `keys` group for `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is refused at load, naming the group to add.
- A `--kill` with `n` counts every crossing of its point and lands at the first eligible one at or after the nth (not while a tool call is between its dispatch and its kept answer), so it always lands when the run reaches the point n times or more; the kill line's `crossing.n` is the ordinal it landed on, `DemoResult.kills` carries `n` and `planned`, and the summary says `planned n=10, landed at n=12` when they differ.
- The place answers where a kill request is written (`Place.requestFile(attempt)`), and the child writes it durably (a synced temporary file, renamed, the directory synced). The supervisor mints a random nonce for each attempt (`KILL_NONCE`, which the child reads once and removes from its environment) and honors a request only with that nonce, writing a `kill.request.ignored` host line for one without it. One fresh run id per invocation (`<demo name>-<8 hex>`, `DemoResult.runId`) reaches the place as `paths.id` and the child as `RUN_ID`, so a second run of one demo on a shared disk never finds the first's run or signals.
- A reference kill takes `where: { field: value }`: it fires only at an announced crossing whose info has those values (`cross(point, { info })`, which the crossing log records), and composes with `n` as the nth matching crossing. `transport.tool.started` fires for every tool, so `{ point: "transport.tool.started", where: { tool: "install" } }` lands on the install step. A storm takes no `where`.
- A `--live` run's judge and model have the crossings the fakes' have (`judge.asked <label>`, `judge.answered <label>`, `model.request turn <n>`, `model.answered turn <n>`, the same names and counting), so a kill lands before a paid question or request is sent, or after its answer and before the run keeps it, and the resume pays the judge once (a kill does not cut a request while the provider has it outstanding).
- `--record` follows the run: `events.jsonl` is appended to as the lines arrive (whole lines, section 3's order, a memo after its commit is complete), blobs are written whole before any line names them, and the manifest is last, so a viewer in follow mode reads a live run and its replay as one file; the finished file equals a fresh export. The manifest's `job` blob is always written when the entry knows the job.
- `LiveParts.tools`, the same name -> function map as `Fakes.tools`: on `--live` the effect door (evidence, receipts, kill crossings) dispatches to it; a tool missing there runs from `fakes.tools` only when demo.json's `tools` entry says `local: "fakes"`, else the call fails `no live source for <tool>`. On fakes nothing changes.
- `--operator-kill <file>`: a control file the supervisor watches. When it appears while a process runs, the supervisor removes it, writes a `kill` host line (`crossing` null, label `operator`), kills through the place and resumes as usual; a kill still planned for the ended attempt moves to the next.
- The reference entry has a place seam: `demo({ place })` takes a `Place` (where the session lives and how its process starts, stops and restarts), and `localPlace` is the child process of this machine. The supervisor decides when to kill and writes the kill line first; the place decides how. A place whose runner opens a claim is handed a fresh mount token on the runner's standard input for each attempt, never in argv or env, and a place that cannot lose its machine refuses a box kill.
- A place whose child writes its recording inside the place brings it home through `Place.recordHome { dir(attempt), bringHome(into, { final }) }` (blobs first, then whole spool lines): the supervisor drives it at each status tick and once final, before the follower closes, the manifest and the release; a failure writes `recording.carry.failed`, refuses the recording only for an attempt that ended on its own, and stands (`lost: true`) for a lost box. The supervisor hands every order `RECORD_STARTED` (the recording's clock) and `RECORD_GEN`; the child's recorder syncs its spool, rolls it into 1 MB segments, and redacts its own machine's host name and boot id with no sign-in file.
- The supervisor writes `run.json` host lines from `place.status()` (any place that has it): the record verbatim with `gen` its generation, when its status, generation, holder or sealedSeq changes, read at a runner's start and exit, a kill and the result, and on a 2 s tick. The recorder replaces `holder.host` and `holder.bootId` with labels (`host-1`, `boot-1`, ...) and redacts this machine's own host name and boot id.
- A place's signals may end with `{ kind: 'signals-failed', code, message }` (or its reader may throw): the supervisor writes a `signals.failed` host line and ends the attempt as after a failed kill, and nothing in the signals pump can end the supervisor. The messages of `kill.failed` and `signals.failed` have any absolute path outside the run's directory replaced by `<path>` and are capped at 240 characters.
- The reference entry takes `sites` from a demo's spec: the agent gets `use_website`, and a task for a site runs a child agent in a browser of its own and answers a short report. A kill while the child works leaves it recorded under its site and request id, and the resumed run finds it instead of starting another.
- The reference entry fetches a demo's `sources.json` (checked files `{ id, url, bytes, sha256, license }`) into the run's `work/` as `<id><extension>`, verified by size and hash, on `--live` and with `--fetch-sources`; a no-key run uses the files the demo commits. A file that is not the checked bytes is refused, naming what was checked and what came.
  A demo whose files depend on the input passes `sourcesFor(input)` (items `{ id, url, bytes, sha256 }`): only those are fetched, and a no-key run that lacks their files is refused in one line naming `--fetch-sources`. Downloads are kept by hash in `--cache <dir>` (default `agentrun-sources` in the OS temp dir), so a re-run does not fetch again; a cached copy that no longer has the checked bytes is fetched again.
  A `sources.json` also lists `pages` (visited live by the agent, with their URLs and hand-check dates): they are passed through untouched, never fetched or refused. A `sources` path starting `examples/` is read from the repository root.
- Code mode in the reference host gains `vision.judge` beside `vision.see` (Jev's typed answers about images, through the host's capped and priced judge seam, with the declaration in the tool's description), and `tools.bash` follows the skills' contract: it resolves the output string, and a command that exits non-zero rejects with an Error whose message names the exit code and holds the output (the sandbox hands a script a thrown error's message only). Code mode also gains `vision.transcribe` (words with times from the host's speech-to-text model, chosen by its config from `TRANSCRIBE_BASE_URL`/`TRANSCRIBE_API_KEY`, `FAL_KEY`, `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY`; the key lives in the adapter, never in the sandbox), filed in `vision.looks` as tool `transcribe`.
- The reference entry files every look the vision guard reports into a `vision.looks` document of the run's store, with the screenshot it was of as a blob and its evidence path as its receipt, so the recording's counters read the eyes' looks and cost; a demo passes only its eyes, from its vision lane (`fakes.vision(host, running)` on fakes, the live parts' on `--live`), and the entry calls a demo's own `onLook` and `onMiss` after its own, never replacing them. `vision.filing` is the first of the packages' own kill crossings: the eyes have answered and the look is not yet filed, so the next process files the kept answer and does not ask the eyes again. A planned kill that never lands is reported (`unlanded`) and the command exits 1.
  A kill entry takes `hold` and `waitMs` at a parking crossing (`vision.filing`): the nth filing and those after it are parked until `hold` are parked or `waitMs` has passed, then the process is killed, so several answers are kept and none filed. A look is kept with its data set item, its miss when it failed and each frame's second, and every read leaves a live `vision`/`shown` line.
- **`apps/viewer`: the document panel draws the PDF pages a result cites, each box over its page**
  - A cited result (`agentrun.cited/1`) whose cites name a blob and a page (`page=N`) has those pages drawn whole with
    PDF.js, each once, and every box a row cites (`page=N&xywh=percent:x,y,w,h`, percent of the page's crop box) placed
    over its page with its row's number (and the row's words where the page is wide enough; rows citing one region share
    a box); the rows that cite a page are listed under it, numbered, "no box" for one without a region.
    A box and a row that cite a look select it, as a citation chip does. A recording whose result cites no page draws no
    document panel.
  - The panel contract gains a `pdf-page` node (the shell draws that page of a PDF blob, its children placed over it in
    percent), `showsResult(result, { blobType })`, which draws a panel for a recording by its result as `shows` does by its
    lines, and `ViewContext.manifestResult`, the manifest's result for a recording in which no line delivers one, which the
    document panel shows at every stop labelled as the run's final result, since no line gives its time.
  - PDF.js (`pdfjs-dist` 6.4.299, Apache-2.0) ships as its release files in `apps/viewer/vendor/pdfjs/`, copied to
    `dist/pdfjs/`, so the viewer still builds with no install; a scan's CCITT, JBIG2 and JPEG 2000 images are decoded by
    its JavaScript decoders (PDFium's, BSD-3-Clause, and OpenJPEG's, BSD-2-Clause), shipped beside it with their
    licenses. The page loads it only to draw a page, with its worker a file of the same origin, and hands it the PDF's
    bytes, so it draws under a policy that loads scripts, workers and data from the page's own origin only; a page opened
    from `file://` says it cannot and links the PDF at its page.
  - A followed run keeps each PDF it read whole, and a dropped folder reads its files directly, so neither fetches a
    `blob:` address for one.
- The run viewer's public replay shows what the run's agent did, and no longer its kills, resumes or receipts by default (`apps/viewer`, PANELS.md "What a public replay shows").
  - **Not drawn by default:** the timeline's process lane with its kills, restores and journal opens (the picture starts below it), the processes, kills, repeated, asked twice and journal opens counters, the chapters made of process starts and kills (in the page and in the embed protocol's `ready`), the vision panel's kill and resume badges and its kept, repeated, asked twice and possible payments meters, the workflow map's kills, cut marks and price of chaos, the transport panel's kills, and the disk panel's generation that ended without a holder. A panel marks such a part with the class `durability`.
  - **One click away:** the strip's looks paid and cost are under a disclosure, Receipts, and so are the vision panel's meters and the receipt of its look.
  - **A look leads with its answer:** the vision panel's film and pages lanes show what the eyes answered and the rows that cite it first, the picture below in a disclosure a click opens.
  - `?debug=durability` draws the view as it was, every part inline. A panel reads it from `ViewContext.debug`.
- `examples/vision-agent` (`npm run demo:vision -- examples/<folder>`): one vision agent for any question about a set of files, on the reference host. It is given the question in plain words and the files, plans its own steps, writes code with `vision.see`, `vision.judge` and `vision.transcribe` following pi-vision's skills as the package ships them, runs gemini-3.8-flash thinking at medium, and ends with an HTML report that cites every claim at the file's own page, second or region; the recording keeps the report and its pictures and clips. Three examples are questions and files, with no code: `examples/video-chapters` and `examples/hear-and-find` (NASA's 1969 Apollo 11 film, fetched and checked) and `examples/document-check` (a Delaware certified copy filed with the SEC and an altered test copy, committed with their source in ASSETS.md).
- `npm run export:source` leaves out only a machine's own `*.local.*` and `*.local` files, as its receipt says. Its rule also dropped any path component named `local` or starting with `local.`, so the source archive was missing `packages/pi-transport/src/box/local.ts`, the file behind pi-transport's `./box/local` export. The reference host imports that file, so the downloaded source did not build (`verify:source`). Across the tracked tree, it is the only file the archive gains.
- `changes.mjs --fold` writes a published package's dated section in one order whether a release folds once or in two commits. A later fold used to append its bullets at the end of the version's section; one fold groups the section by kind and orders it by fragment name. When the section is exactly an earlier fold's output (its fragments gone from the tree, present at the merge base), the fold now rebuilds it from the merge base's text with the earlier and the new fragments together in fold order. A section edited by hand since is left as it was and the new bullets go at its end (`--check` reads only Unreleased sections, so it does not look at a dated one). This extends the held-package rule to published changelogs.
- The hero's kill-beat test claims only what holds under any schedule: at most two processes fetched, the killed one at least the three published figures it had kept, and nine fetches in all. It no longer fails when the nine branches fetch before the third kept crossing is taken alone.
- A recorded blob is written to a temporary file in the recording's `.spool/`, synced and renamed into `blobs/`, so a viewer following a live run never fetches a half-written one and a killed write leaves no torn blob; the validator and the gate are unchanged. `browser: false` in demo.json means the entry creates no browser, live or on fakes (a `--live` run then needs no Browserbase key and no Chrome), and a page call fails with `this demo has no browser`.
- Every node of a workflow run by the reference host thinks: its own `thinking` level, else `low` (the DSL runner's default), never `off` (a node that carries `off` runs at `low`).
- `--record` takes the exact secret values out of a recording: the values of the environment names the demo declares, and the sign-in's tokens (the access, refresh and id token strings and an API-key credential's key from `auth.json` under `$AGENTRUN_HOME`, else `~/.config/agentrun`). Every recorded line, text blob and the manifest has them replaced, a sign-in file that cannot be read fails the record, and the finished recording is scanned and refused if a value slipped through, naming the key and the file and never the value.
  A refreshed token is redacted too (the sign-in file is read again when its mtime or size changes, and the redaction uses every value seen), the finished-recording scan reads the file once more, and the child is handed `AGENTRUN_HOME` so it uses the file the recorder reads.
- The reference recorder takes the run's own machine out of every line and text blob, beside the exact secret values: the user name (as a whole token, in a path segment or an `ls -l` owner or group column, never as a word in a sentence), the home directory (`~`) and the paths under the run's directories (`<run>/...`). A tool result that listed a directory no longer carries the box's user name into a recording.
- The verify job's time limit is 40 minutes (it was 25). A run on main takes 17 to 24 minutes today, so a slow runner was cut at the limit and the check failed without a test failing.
- The source export carries a PDF only when one line of `ASSETS.md` names the file, in backticks, together with its SHA-256. Any other PDF stops the export with "Unreviewed PDF: <path> is not in ASSETS.md with its SHA-256 <sha>", so every binary in the source archive is still one someone reviewed. `.pdf` joins the binary types the export knows; the other binary types are unchanged.
- `docs/examples.md` lists the two examples that already use pi-vision, the reference host (`npm run demo:reference`) and the hero job (`npm run demo:hero`), and names the three examples that are to come (video chapters, hear and find, document verification), one line each. Each gets a row in the table when it lands.
- The hero's README and `demo.json` claim say what the agent does (reads nine grid operators' charts with vision, each number cited to its screenshot). The kill-and-resume beat stays as a test, with a short "For developers" section in the README.
- Three more questions for the vision agent, each a folder with no code: `examples/apollo-descent` (NASA's Apollo 11 transcript, photographs and film, minute by minute), `examples/boulder-chapters` (a 1930s government film in chapters) and `examples/fair-robot` (a robot first seen in a 1939 film). `docs/examples.md` lists all six vision examples; `ASSETS.md` records each film's source and licence.

## 0.1.0-beta.16 (2026-10-18)

- The live server's page also lists the agent's saved sign-ins by site (`GET /agent`, never a ref) and says when a run started signed in; the live browser floats over the page, opening large while the run waits for the person; and once a run is not live its Browser tab plays back what its browser showed, recorded owner-only beside the run with nothing kept while the person signs in, and never part of a replay.
- **Reference example: one scanner, and `--record` scans before it finishes**
  - `examples/reference/scan.ts` is the one scan for what a published recording must never hold: a credential's shape
    or a machine path. A copied example keeps it.
  - The recorder's `finishRecording` scans the recording's lines, its text blobs and the manifest's own text before it
    writes the manifest. A finding throws, naming the shape and the file, and no manifest is written, so the directory
    is never a finished recording.
  - `scripts/recording/validate.mjs` imports the same scanner instead of its own copy, and re-exports it, so the
    validator and the hosted gallery's gate refuse exactly what the recorder refuses.
  - The scanner refuses a JSON Web Token (`jwt`: a base64url JSON header and payload and a signature, the shape of a
    ChatGPT sign-in's access and id tokens) and a Browserbase API key (`bb_live_`, the prefix Browserbase's docs show), in
    a line, a blob or the manifest, and behind a JSON escape too. The source export keeps its own list
    (`scripts/export-source.mjs`), unchanged.
  - The hosted gallery's gate scans every published text file as the recorder does, raw and decoded when it is JSON, so
    an escaped credential in a blob no line names is found too.
- **`apps/viewer`: box time in the run's cost, and a scripted run's real parts on the strip (RECORDING.md drafts 33, 35, 36)**
  - The cost sums each `box.receipt` host line's `cost_usd`, once per box by its label, and labels it at list price:
    `$0.0147 (boxes $0.0047 at list price)`. A restore keeps it: a box's time was spent whatever the store went back to.
  - The cost is unknown while a box pi-transport's own lines show started (`box.created`) has no receipt of its own
    (source `transport`), when a receipt's `cost_usd` is null (a kind with no published price), and, as before, when the
