    recording has no durable line. Started boxes named by label (`box-N`, as a receipt names its box) are matched to their
    own receipts; ones named by the provider's id, which no receipt carries, are counted against the transport receipts.
  - A scripted run whose boxes cost something reads `scripted lead, boxes $0.0047 at list price`, its other parts named
    scripted with their sum when they priced anything, never `scripted, $0`. A scripted run with no box, or whose boxes
    cost nothing, reads as before.
  - The counters carry `costParts`, the cost by part (`lead`, `judge`, `eyes`, `browser`, `boxes`), and `costLabel` moves
    to `./counters.ts`. The transport panel's cost row uses it, so it reads as the strip.
- The run viewer draws the pictures a cited result names (`apps/viewer`): a row of an `agentrun.cited/1` result that cites a file which is a picture in the recording's `blobs/` (PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF) shows it as a small thumbnail, several side by side; any other file stays a link. A window of film the eyes saw as video, with no picture of its own, shows the keyframes the rows cite beside it, each captioned as that chapter's keyframe and noted as not what the eyes saw; a page keeps its own scan. Pictures come only from the recording: read from a server (`?src=`, `?follow=`), where `blobs/` cannot be listed, a blob no line names (a picture only the result cites) is looked for by its name the first time a view draws it, and reads loading until then. A result's JSON is read without fetching a `data:` or `blob:` URL (an embedded site decodes it in the page; a followed run keeps what it read), so a site served with `connect-src 'self'` still draws the result. A missed tile in the photographs lane is labelled by its miss code. A panel's view reads a blob's media type through `ViewContext.blobType`.
- **`apps/viewer`: the run's picture (RECORDING.md draft 31)**
  - The picture panel draws the latest `picture` host line's image at or before the stop, a gap before the first, and the
    last one after the run ends. A recording with no `picture` line draws no picture panel; one with pictures shows it
    first.
  - The viewer's format check takes a `picture` line, which names no process (no `data.gen`), and checks its `blob` (a
    SHA-256 in `blobs/`), `label` (a string) and `commit` (a whole number).
  - `apps/viewer/RECORDING.md` follows the format's draft 31: the host row gains the supervisor's failure lines and the
    `picture` line.
- **`apps/viewer`: a message's text parts are kept apart, and a prompt's footer folds**
  - `textOf` joined a message's text blocks with nothing, so a second text part (a footer a job adds after its prompt) ran
    into the first. Text parts are now joined with a blank line (`textPartsOf` gives them in order).
  - The model turns panel shows the prompts (user messages) too: a prompt shows the start of its first text part, and
    everything else, the rest of that part and every later part (a footer after the prompt), folds whole under one
    "more"; a short prompt of one part is shown as it is.
- `examples/web`: a run in an imported home reads its timeline from its store. A pack carries no `run.json`, so the runs server answered 409 ("the run is live") for every run of a home made by `importPack`; a run whose entry in the home's index has an `endedAt` was carried back at its source, so the server now treats it as released with or without `run.json` and gives its timeline, state and cost from its store. A run with no `endedAt` and no `run.json` is still not read from its store.
- examples/web: a message that comes after a run answered, between its instance's exit and its carry back, waits for the carry back and gets `409 RUN_FINISHED`. Before, it started the run again, and once the carry back landed that instance was never stopped, so it stayed up with its browser. An instance still up for a run that is already carried back (by another writer of the home) is now sent SIGTERM.
- `changes.mjs --fold` writes the same held-package changelog when a release folds in two commits as when it folds once. A held package's Unreleased section keeps what it had; the bullets of this release's fragments follow it in the fold's order (kind, then file name). When a release folds, merges main and folds a late fragment again, the earlier fold's run is rebuilt from the merge base's text with the late fragments in that order, so `--check` accepts it. `--base <ref>` (default `origin/main`) names what the merge base is taken against; a section that is not an earlier fold's exact output is left as it was and the new bullets are appended after it.
- **`scripts/recording/validate.mjs` and `apps/viewer`: two recordings that follow the format are no longer refused**
  - An inline image is found by its structure, never by the length of a string. An image block that holds its `data`,
    and a `data:image/` URL, are still refused: an image belongs in `blobs/`. A run of 4000 or more base64 characters
    anywhere else (a store's chunk, a checkpoint) is data and is accepted, so a writer no longer cuts it up to pass.
  - A restore whose pi committed once at open is accepted (RECORDING.md draft 37). Its process may start at `head + 1`
    when its own snapshot is at commit `head`, since pi commits once at open when a generation was running and that
    commit's state is in the snapshot. Both the viewer's check ("the restore's head is not the process's first new
    commit") and the validator's continuity check refused it. A first new commit above `head + 1`, `head + 1` without
    the snapshot at `head`, and every move fail as before.
- The reference recorder writes each blob to a temporary file in the recording's `.spool/`, fsyncs it and renames it into `blobs/` whole, so a process killed while it writes (the budget stop, a planned kill) leaves a temporary file in `.spool/` and never a torn blob under a name that says it is whole. The validator and the gallery gate are unchanged: `.spool/` is never published.
- **`apps/viewer`: a build skips a blob still being written**
  - A recorder writes a blob as `.<sha>.<ext>.tmp-<pid>` and renames it once whole, so a copy of a recording taken while it
    goes may hold such a name in `blobs/`. `build.mjs` neither lists it in the gallery (embedded or `--files`) nor copies
    it, so the build of such a copy passes the hosted gallery's gate.
- Two notes for host authors. The `pi-agent-learn` README says how a judge on a general model, rather than Jev, answers
  curate's questions: ask for `{ answer, confidence }` and derive the yes-probability, since a model asked for a bare
  probability reads it as its confidence. The host integration guide says to give each tool a workflow calls through
  `createWorkflowTool` a `resultSchema`: without one, `describe` reports pi's result envelope and the result reaches the
  next node untyped.
- The `examples/agent-learn/` demo now shows a workflow earning its place. After run 2's workflow is admitted as a trial,
  runs 5 to 7 are offered it, load it by name and run it with the DSL's `agentrun` tool; `finishRun` proposes their uses from the
  records the tool left in each run's own store, and curation promotes the trial to active on the third clean run (a run counts once, however many cases it ran). Run 8 saves a
  second revision that hands an issue with no body to a person; runs 9 and 10 escalate at its gate, so curation demotes it and
  the first revision is active again, and a run that saves the demoted document again proposes nothing. The README has a new
  section, "A workflow earns its place", with the rules: three clean uses promote, two failed or escalated uses in a row demote
  and roll back, and a demoted revision never returns. The tests read each use from the tool's own records.
- New example `examples/agent-learn/`: an issue-triage agent that learns in its own git repository, end to end in one command
  with a scripted model and judge (`npm run demo:learn`, no key, no network). The agent's `AGENTS.md` says
  to call `learn` with type correction when a maintainer corrects it. In run 1 a maintainer gives a project rule no model can
  guess; the host proposes the note on `runs/run-1`, and curation merges it to `main`. Run 2's first model request carries it
  in `<learned>`; run 2 applies it, marks it used, and saves a workflow with the DSL's `agentrun` tool, which curation admits
  as a trial skill. `retireLearning` then takes the rule out (a `git revert` would conflict once the rule was used), so run 3
  labels without it and is offered the trial, and a run that tries to keep a host secret proposes nothing.
  `host.mjs` is the reference host loop over `@parcha/pi-git-remote`, `@parcha/pi-agent-learn` and
  `@parcha/pi-durable-agent`: `prepareRun` (fetch main, refresh the agent home at its sha with a compare-and-swap, start the
  run there, seed its section and its trials), `finishRun` (carry back, then propose notes, saved workflows and uses) and
  `curateNow` (the judge is the DSL's `runJudge` port). `--live` runs the triage runs on a model at an OpenAI Responses
  endpoint.

## 0.1.0-beta.15 (2026-10-16)

- The `agentrun` tool dry-runs the inspected workflow: its `dry-run` action (with an optional `input`), the `/agentrun dry-run` command and the inspector's Dry-run action run it on synthetic values, with no model, Jev or tool call, no run journal, and the last run's receipt left as it was. The result's `status` is `passed`, `not_run` (a check could not run and `skipped` says why; it proves nothing) or `failed` (`problems` say why). A workflow with code nodes is `not_run` until the user issues `/agentrun dry-run --trusted`, and a code node that throws is reported by its label, without what it threw. The tool's parameters gain the `dry-run` action, and the packaged skill tells the agent to dry-run a workflow before it runs it.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable` gives a pi-durable session the agentrun tool. `createWorkflowTool(host)` returns one extension that carries the tool and the workflow task: the host installs it and lists the tool's name in the agent definition. Its describe, inspect, dry-run, save, load and list are the coding-agent tool's, run by the same code, over the project store `host.projectDir` names. Its run starts the inspected draft as one background `agentrun.workflow` task of the calling conversation, writes the run's progress to the call's running output, and answers with the outcome. The same draft and input asked for again, after a restart too, reach the same run and make no effect twice; `again: true` starts a fresh run of them, which a later run reaches. Code nodes run only through the host's `runCode`. A workflow uses only the tools the host registers in `tools`, as a call's tool and as an agent node's tools, and inspect names any other. A root agent offers the tools of the extensions it lists: list the tool's extension beside any other the conversation needs. `createWorkflowExtension`'s `start` and `read` take a tool's `api` as their caller (`WorkflowCaller`), naming the conversation in `options.conversationId`.
- New entry `@parcha/agentrun-pi/signin`: sign in, pick a model, check keys, for a demo or your own app before its first model call. `@earendil-works/pi-ai` is a new optional peer at `*`; only this entry loads it, and every other entry imports and runs without it.
  - `checkKeys(spec, options)` reports what a run needs and what is missing (keys by env name and where to create them, programs on `PATH` with their platforms and minimum versions, packages with the exact `npm install --no-save` line), with exit code 2 and no request sent when a required item is missing. `checkSetupPrompt(spec, text)` checks a demo's setup prompt against its spec, and `scripts/check-setup-prompts.mjs` runs it in CI.
  - `resolveModel(spec, options)`: an explicit provider and model with that provider's env key wins; else a stored ChatGPT sign-in with the model chosen from the account's `GET /v1/models` by capability; else `signIn`. The eyes model resolves the same way or is refused naming the gap.
  - `signIn(options)` runs pi-ai's own Sign in with ChatGPT with this app's name and a persisted host id, and fails at once with a clear message when port 1455 is taken. It refuses a pi-ai that ignores the app name (before 1.1).
  - The token lives in `$AGENTRUN_HOME/auth.json` (default `~/.config/agentrun/`, directory 0700, file 0600, written whole and renamed). Refreshes are serialized across processes by an operating-system lock (an exclusive SQLite transaction on `auth.json.lock`) that a killed process never leaves behind, so a rotating refresh token is never sent twice.
  - The ChatGPT plan's usage limit maps to `UsageLimitError` and is never retried.
- `SystemOneQuestion` and `SystemOneAnswer` carry the Box question (`{ type: "box", instructions }` answered by `{ type: "box", box: { top, bottom, left, right }, confidence }`, the edges fractions of the image's height and width), new type `SystemOneBox`, and a response reason `box_coordinates`. `validateAnswers` refuses edges outside [0, 1], a box whose top or left edge lies past its bottom or right, or a confidence outside [0, 1] (a missing one is `null`); `answerConfidence` reads a `null` confidence as none; `synthesizeAnswers` and `answersToValue` handle it. A Box is a host-side question: no workflow node declares one and the validator is unchanged.
- `dryRunWorkflow` is bounded and can be stopped. It stops after `maxSteps` synthetic steps (an agent's or a judge's answer, an effect, a code node the host's `runCode` runs; 10,000 by default) or when its `signal` ends, and answers `ok: true` with `skipped` giving the reason. It also yields to the event loop every 64 steps, so a host takes a stop meanwhile. Before, a loop whose synthetic answers never met its `until` ran to its `maxIters` (which has no ceiling) and held the event loop for the whole time: a `maxIters` of 200,000 took 5 to 21 seconds, during which no timer ran.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/graph`, an import-free subpath: `workflowGraph(workflow)` returns the nodes (JSON Pointer path, kind, label, `via` for a call) and edges (`next`, `branch`, `route`, `item`, `child`) of a workflow document from its JSON alone, and `nodeStatus(graph, source)` says for each node whether a run has it `done`, `working`, `failed` or `not-run`, from the recovery driver's frame and the map items' records. A map that ended on an item's failure (`pin.partial`) is failed, and a node inside a map's or loop's body is not done before the map or loop is. `nodeStatus(graph, source, { item })` reads one map item's (or loop iteration's) record on its own: that item is the instance, so its body is done when the item is.
  The pi session's text view now takes its node list from `workflowGraph`, so the terminal view and a graph panel cannot disagree about which nodes a workflow has.
  The subpath's fixtures (`packages/dsl/test/fixtures/graph`: a sequence, a parallel, a route, a nested map with a child workflow, and a map in each layout, each finished and cut, with the driver's frame and the map items' records) come from `generate.mjs`, so a panel's test reads what a real run kept.
  One recording of the nested shape in the keyed layout, cut by a kill and resumed (`packages/dsl/test/fixtures/graph/nested.recording.jsonl`, the store's durable lines), comes from `examples/fixtures/graph-nested/run.ts` through the reference entry; a test reads it back through `nodeStatus` at every commit. The reference entry's run options (and a demo's) take an optional `layout` (`v4` keeps the map items as `agentrun.items` documents); a `Job` is unchanged.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl` exports `Job`, `{ workflow: Workflow; input: Record<string, unknown>; files?: Record<string, Uint8Array> }`, the shape a host passes when it runs a workflow with the input and files it starts with, and `defineJob(job)`, an identity that checks that shape and keeps the workflow's literal type (a workflow declared `as const satisfies Workflow` flows through). New public exports; no behavior or stored format changes.
- The `examples/web` run page reads only through a `RunSource`, so the same page shows a live server's runs (`httpSource`) and a replay from static files (`staticSource`). `mountRunPage(root, source, { theme })` shows the ways to act and the live browser only when the source has them, and builds no absolute URL, so it works under any path; it draws light, dark or the system's (`auto`, the default) from `--ar-*` color tokens a host can override. `writeReplay` is the one writer of a replay's static layout, refusing ids and file paths a page could not ask for, a summary for another id and runs whose paths collide, and swapping the new replay in so a failed write leaves the old one whole, and `pack-page.mjs` writes the self-contained replay page with its Content-Security-Policy in a meta tag. A replay names a dollar figure only for a recording whose manifest says its providers are `real`: otherwise its cost is `null`, its usage rows count model requests with no amount, and the page's header shows no figure.
- **`@parcha/agentrun-jev`: images and Box questions, on `@typesafe-ai/sdk` 0.7.0**
  - `createJevRunner` takes images (`assets`: a reference to PNG or JPEG file bytes and their type, the reference written in the state or a question) and Box questions (Jev 1.14). It sends `TypeSafe-Danger-Zone-Images: true` on every request that carries an image or a Box and on no other, as a multipart request. Priced, receipted and retried as text is; `request_sha256` names each image by its own digest.
  - Refused before any request: a Box on a model that does not answer it (name `jev-1.14.0`; the default `jev-latest` is 1.13), a Box with no image, a file that is not PNG or JPEG or is over 16 MiB, a reference nothing uses, and, for `provider: "openrouter"`, anything that carries an image or a Box. `model` can be named per call.
  - New types `JevJudge` (what `createJevRunner` returns), `JevJudgeParams`, `JevJudgeResult` and `JevImage`: the port a host hands to a package that asks Jev about images without importing the SDK.
  - `@typesafe-ai/sdk` 0.6.0 to 0.7.0. The SDK now compresses request bodies with zstd by default; the runner turns that off, so bodies are the uncompressed JSON they were.
- `createJevRunner` can reach Jev through OpenRouter: `provider: "openrouter"` (default `"typesafe"`, unchanged) sends to OpenRouter's System One path (`https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/systemone`, or an explicit `baseURL`) with `~typesafe/jev-latest` as the default model, and takes its key from `apiKey` or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`; it never falls back to `TYPESAFE_API_KEY`. `jevProviderFromEnv(env)` returns `"openrouter"` only when `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is set and `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is not. The caps (`timeoutMs`, `maxStateBytes`, retries) are unchanged. `cost_usd` is the provider's `usage.cost` when reported, else the configured `pricing` estimate, else `null`, never 0, and a `usage` with a cost and no token counts is now accepted (it returns `usage: null`). `provider` cannot be combined with `client`. New public API; no stored format changes.
- **`@parcha/agentrun-pi/workflows`: save, load and list saved workflows against a directory the host passes**
  - `WorkflowStore` (with `WorkflowStoreError`, `StoredWorkflow` and `WorkflowRevision`) is the store the `/agentrun` extension keeps for its project
    directory, now a public entry that loads nothing from pi. `new WorkflowStore(projectDirectory)` takes a project directory, never the workflows
    directory itself: records live in `<projectDirectory>/.pi/agentrun/workflows/<name>/`, so the extension (which keeps using its `ctx.cwd`) and a host
    that passes the same directory read each other's records. `save(name, workflow)` publishes an immutable revision, `load(name, digest?)` returns
    the given revision or the last one saved, and `list()` returns every revision, newest first.
  - Nothing about the format or the checks changes: real directories and regular files only (a symbolic link in the path or among the records is refused
    with `unsafe_path`), the record's metadata and its SHA-256 are checked on every load, and a `<name>/` directory copied into the workflows
    directory loads.
- The recovery driver can keep a run in a keyed layout (`agentrun.frozen_run.v4`), so a map's commits stay linear in its items. Opt in with `RecoveryOptions.layout: "v4"` (for a pi task host, return it from `WorkflowTaskHost.options`); the default in this release is `"v3"`, which keeps the driver's whole state in one record rewritten at every commit. On the reference job, a map of 400 one-call items wrote 2.007 GB in 643 s in `"v3"`, and 10.6 MB in 20 s in `"v4"`.
  - What lies in each map item is kept in that item's record beside the state, each committed state inside an item as a delta against its map's base, checked against its digest when it is rebuilt (a state that does not rebuild refuses the open with `FROZEN_CHECKPOINT_INVALID`), and each state the driver references once by its digest, a failed map's finished results included. In the pi document store these are two new document families, `agentrun.items` (one per map item, keyed by the journal key and the item's path) and `agentrun.states` (one per state, keyed by the journal key and its digest); `agentrun.driver` keeps its record shape and its `generation`.
  - Only a store that keeps parts can hold the keyed layout: its opened journal has `items(paths)` and `stateOf(sha256)`, and every write takes the parts that go with its state (`RecoveryJournal`'s `save`, `admit` and `complete` gain an optional `parts`, `admit` an optional `owner`). The memory, file and pi document stores keep them. On a store without them the driver keeps `"v3"`, says why once per open in a new note kind, `layout`, and reports it as `driver.layout`. The store conformance suite (`@parcha/agentrun-dsl/recovery/testing`) gains three cases for parts.
  - `itemState(item, states, path)` (`@parcha/agentrun-dsl/recovery`) rebuilds a map item's state at a path from its `agentrun.items` value and the `agentrun.states` values by digest, as the driver does, and checks it against that path's digest; it returns `{ state }`, or `{ unreadable }` with the reason when the base is missing or a digest differs, never a guess. A reader of a recording calls it rather than reimplement the rebuild; the SHA-256 is Node's unless the caller passes its own synchronous `{ sha256 }`, and the module that defines it imports nothing, so a reader with no Node can carry it whole. `partialResults(partial, states)`, beside it, reads a failed map's finished results from the driver state's `pin.partial`: in the keyed layout one digest names the list of slots, each `null` or the digest of that item's result, and each slot comes back as `null`, `{ result }` or `{ unreadable }`.
  - A run in the keyed layout stays in it whatever a later process asks for. A run stored whole moves to it at its first write that knows the base of every map still running (a map names its base when it resumes, a moment after the open). After the move, the checkpoint bases of `agentrun.driver` keep the ids of the effects admitted before it: a cost bounded by the work done before the move, which later commits do not grow; each commit after the move costs the same however many items the map has.
  - A driver that knows only `"v3"`, from an earlier release, refuses a run in the keyed layout (`FROZEN_CHECKPOINT_INVALID`) rather than run it with its items missing. 0.1.0-beta.13 and earlier fail their own task on a v4 run and report it refused; that ends the run's agent nodes in flight, and the run then fails on this release too, with no call repeated and nothing corrupted. So opt in only when no process on an earlier release can open the run. The default moves to `"v4"` in a later release.
  - From this release, a run stored in a schema newer than the build knows is refused with its own code, `RECOVERY_SCHEMA_NEWER` (the error names the schema it `found` and the ones it knows, `known`), and the pi workflow task leaves that run pending, never terminal: the process's waiters get the refusal, and a build that knows the schema continues the run. A checkpoint the driver cannot read (`FROZEN_CHECKPOINT_INVALID`) still ends the task.
  - `validateWorkflow` results carry an optional `warnings` list that never refuses a workflow. Its first warning is a map with no `resultPath` whose body writes no `as`: each item's result is its whole state, the mapped list included, so the map's result grows with the square of its items. The author skill says the same.
- The reference entry honors `Fakes.extensions` and `Fakes.vision(host, running)` on a no-key run, takes a `result(record, { dir, keep })` hook that writes the recording's result (`agentrun.cited/1`) from what a demo makes, hands tools `ToolContext.live`, uses a local box instead of Docker on fakes (Docker only with `--live`), and takes `--input <name>` for a capture named in `demo.json`'s `input.fixtures` (a map from a name to a JSON file under `examples/<demo>/fixtures/`, read as the input); on fakes a name that is not a capture is refused with the list of captures, and a path outside `fixtures/` is refused.
  A live child is given exactly the environment names `demo.json` declares (keys, `oneOf` members, programs) and nothing undeclared; `LiveParts.vision` takes `(host, running)` like `fakes.vision`.
- The reference example is now a platform for demos: `demo({ self, spec, job, fakes })` reads the standard flags (`--live`, `--kill`, `--record`, `--input`, `--max-usd`, `--local-chrome`, `--dir`, `--json`) and runs a spec's `kills` through a supervisor. A kill is a named crossing, a storm, or a box kill (`kind: "box"`), and may target a tool's box (`target: "tool"`) instead of the run's process. The spend cap is metered on the recording and stops a run past it. A live run drives Browserbase when its key is set, and a local Chrome only when asked for. Every demo's judge passes one seam with Jev's caps (50 requests, 40,000 tokens, `JEV_CAP_REACHED`) and Jev's public list price. The entry hands a demo a box provider with `transport.box.*` crossings and transport box lines built in; the crossings are listed in `examples/reference/README.md`.
- A run's result can be the generic cited result, `agentrun.cited/1` (the recording format's own shape): rows of a label, a value and an optional note, each citing a file in `blobs/`, a vision look by its key, or both, with where in it (`page=`, `t=`, `xywh=percent:`), and the page it was read from and when. The vision panel reads it beside `apollo11.account/1`: a row a line, a look's chip opens its receipt and moves its lane (drawing the row's box over a page), a file's chip opens the file (at the cite's fragment, when it has one), a page known only by its address (`url`, read at `at`) is linked and marked not kept, and a row that cites nothing says so. A blob link node may carry a `fragment` the shell appends to its URL, and a new link node `{ tag: 'a', href, text }` names a page outside the recording, linked only when it is an http or https address. Fields the format does not name are the run's own and ignored.
- **`apps/viewer`: embed mode**
  - `&embed=1` frames the viewer in another page: no header (a recording that breaks the format still says so) and the
    full width of its frame.
  - Framed, it speaks a `postMessage` protocol (`PANELS.md`, "Embedding"). It posts `ready` (its stops and chapters),
    `position` at each stop (stop, gen, commit, t and chapter) and `focus`. It obeys `seek` (a stop, a commit of a
    generation, or a time), `focus` (a workflow path) and `theme` (light, dark or auto), each field checked, and only from
    the framing page at the origin its URL names with `&parent=<origin>`; without it, the viewer listens to nothing.
- The run viewer follows a run while it goes (`apps/viewer`, `?follow=<url>`): given a recording directory served over HTTP that grows (`events.jsonl` gaining whole lines, read from the last byte with a `Range` request; each blob in `blobs/` before a line names it; `manifest.json` written last, once the run finished), it reads what is new about once a second, places it in the format's file order (the order a recorder's merge writes), and redraws every panel keeping the reader's place, with the time cursor on the newest line unless the reader scrubbed back. Kills and later processes arrive as lines like any other, and a file the writer replaced whole is read again from its start (each read compares the last bytes it read, and every thirtieth read all of them). A page opened before the run wrote anything, or while its server does not answer, waits and retries; a blob whose download breaks off is asked for again without losing the lines that name it, and once read it is drawn (and a result in it read) with no new line; a reader who scrubbed back stays at their time when a line arrives before it. Each blob is hashed by the browser where the page has its digest, else a MiB at a time, so a large video does not hold the page. While the run goes, its cost reads unknown and a blob not read yet shows as loading; a URL on another server is refused. When the manifest appears, the page reads the file whole, as a fresh load does, checks it against the format, and plays it as any recording. A panel's view is told when the run it draws is still going (`ViewContext.following`).
- **`apps/viewer`: the browser's page actions in repeated and asked twice**
  - The counters strip folds the browser's replay-unsafe page actions (RECORDING.md section 6, draft 25) from the session
    records' `pendingEffect` entries and the `prior` ones they carry, per `(conv, tool, codeSha)`. A dispatch after one
    that reached the page (`finished`, or a sent row that was not denied) is one extra: in repeated when its process is
    later, in asked twice when it is the same. A dispatch after ones that never showed reaching the page is possible,
    shown as "+ N possible" beside repeated and never counted.
  - A recording whose `browser.sessions` lines all carry no `t` (a bare export's) shows page actions not measured, and
    repeated and asked twice read "not measured" rather than 0 when nothing else measured adds to them.
  - `apps/viewer/RECORDING.md` names that signal in the format's own words: a recording whose `browser.sessions` lines all
    carry no `t` (a bare export's) shows page actions not measured, never 0.
- `resolveModel`'s `signal` now reaches a first-time sign-in. Cancelling model setup during the sign-in ends it at once: `resolveModel` rejects with the signal's reason, and pi-ai's callback server lets go of port 1455. Before, the sign-in kept waiting for the browser with the port held, so the next sign-in in that process failed with "Port 1455 is in use". A cancel also ends the sign-in when the interaction's prompt does not watch its own signal.
- `/agentrun status` names the supported Pi range, `>=1.0.4 <1.4.0` (the range of the package's pi-durable and chord peers), and flags only a host outside it, suggesting the newest version CI tests: `npm install -g @earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@1.3.0`. It compared the host with one version, 1.1.0, so it flagged every supported host but 1.1.0 and told it to install 1.1.0. A host outside the range is still not blocked.
- An agent node in flight when its workflow task failed is run again when a later start replaces the failed task, instead of failing the run. The node's conversation belongs to the task that started it; when that task ends, pi aborts the request in flight, and a later start runs a new task over the same journal. The node runner used to find the old conversation, receive the aborted request back, and fail the node for good (`NODE_NOT_DELIVERED`, "the model did not answer (aborted)"), so any path that failed a task with an agent node in flight lost the run at its next start: an open the driver refused, a host whose options threw. Now an attempt whose conversation another task owns runs again in a conversation of the task that runs it, under the same attempt, unless the old conversation already delivered the node's record, which is then the node's. The model is asked again; no call the workflow admitted is made twice.
  - `reuseReceipts`' `sessionId` is optional: absent, each call's own conversation is its session, so a node run again in another conversation after its task was replaced is answered from the receipt the aborted conversation paid for and does not pay again. A host that passes its own `sessionId` keeps it; one that keys by the node's attempt would pay again here.
- `readJournal(file, key).effects` lists every effect of a journal in the keyed layout (`agentrun.frozen_run.v4`). It listed only the ones the driver's record keeps, so a reader of a v4 run missed every outside call made inside a map, unknown ones included, and nested maps' too. Each map item's record lists the effects it owns, and they are now read from there: every effect once, in the order they were admitted, as the whole layout lists them. A resume was never affected: the recovery driver reads the items itself. `state` stays the driver's own record; in the keyed layout what lies in each map item is in that item's record.
- The reference entry's boxes test no longer flakes under load: when the demo plans to lose the install box, the install tool's first command waits until it is killed (no timer), so the loss always lands mid-command and the second box always runs; before, a slow loss let the first command finish and the second box was never made.
- **`apps/viewer`: only the host lines that name a process must carry `data.gen`**
  - The viewer's format check asked every supervisor (`host`) line for `data.gen`, so a valid recording with a `picture`
    or a `box.receipt` line showed a broken-format banner. It now asks only the kinds whose fields name the process
    (`process.started`, `kill`, `result`, `kill.failed`, `kill.request.ignored`, `signals.failed`,
    `recording.carry.failed`, `run.json`, `delegation`, `revoke`, `fenced`); a host kind it does not know is not checked
    for it.
- The stranger job's `registry` mode, which installs `@parcha/pi-durable-disk@beta` from the registry with the README's own
  install line as written and runs the README's quickstart, is blocking: 0.1.0-beta.14, whose peer ranges accept pi below
  1.4, installs with it, where 0.1.0-beta.13 (ranges below 1.3) failed with ERESOLVE once pi 1.3.0 shipped.
- **`apps/viewer`: the embed tests can no longer hold a CI job to its limit**
  - `test/embed.test.ts` read its pages with `--dump-dom` and `--virtual-time-budget`, which now and then never finishes,
    through `spawnSync`, whose timeout sends SIGTERM and then waits for the process to exit with the event loop blocked: a
    Chrome that did not die held `npm run test:viewer` until the job's limit, and no test timeout could fire. Every embed
    test now drives Chrome over the DevTools protocol in real time, through `test/driven-page.ts` (the module the follow
    tests use): every wait bounded and named (the launch, each request, the socket's opening, each page evaluation, a page
    drawing), an answer that can no longer come (the socket closed, Chrome exited) failing at once, and the test's signal
    killing Chrome's process group at once.
  - Every embed test has a timeout in seconds, and so do its cleanups, which close every Chrome (through `closeAll`) before
    removing its files, and the file ends checking that no browser was left open.
    `test/chrome-bounds.test.ts` shows the bounds on a stand-in Chrome that ignores SIGTERM and leaves a helper running.
- **The hosted gallery ships its recordings as files**
  - `.github/workflows/pages.yml` builds the viewer with `--files`: the recordings are published as files with
    `recordings/index.json`, and the page fetches a run only when it is opened, instead of loading every run's lines and
    blobs in `gallery.js`.
  - `scripts/recording/gate.mjs` checks a files build: `recordings/index.json` lists exactly the recordings beside it,
    and the index is the only file beside the recordings.
  - The gate holds every gallery entry, in either build, to exactly what its recording's files give, since the page plays
    the gallery's copy: the same manifest as its `manifest.json`, every blob in its directory and no other, each under its
    own SHA-256 (a files build names the blob's path; an embedded one carries its bytes, which must be the file's), and,
    embedded, the same lines as its `events.jsonl`. A swapped, missing or extra blob, another manifest or other lines are
    refused.

## 0.1.0-beta.14 (2026-10-16)

- Accept pi 1.3: the `@earendil-works/pi-durable` and `@earendil-works/chord` peer ranges now end below 1.4, with the floors unchanged (1.0.4, and 1.1.0 for `@parcha/pi-browser`). pi 1.3.0 pins its own packages to exact versions, so an install is all 1.2 or all 1.3; CI runs every package's whole suite on a 1.3.0 family as well as on 1.2.0.
  - On pi-ai 1.3, a tool call whose arguments are not a JSON object reaches no tool: pi-durable refuses it and shows the model the text it sent. A durable node's `submit` then spends none of its submissions. Before 1.3 the tool ran with `{}`, so `submit` answered with the schema's violations and counted one submission.
- **`apps/viewer`: one disk panel for every demo that runs on a disk place**
  - A lane per claim generation of the run (who held it, from when, and how it ended: released with its sealed sequence, killed or its box taken away, fenced, or replaced between two polls), the claim's events in order (the orphan flag as seen, the revoke and the takeover time, the fence), and each paid call marked made once, repeated, cut by a kill or in flight, from the shell's own counter fold.
  - It reads the supervisor's `host` lines `run.json` (the package's `RunRecord` verbatim), `delegation`, `revoke` and `fenced`, which `RECORDING.md` section 5 now describes (draft 26). A recording with no disk line is unchanged. Viewer only: no package export.
- **`apps/viewer`: the gallery bundles two recorded runs**
  - `reference-job` and `ten-kills-seed21`: the reference example's job with scripted providers (no key, $0), once
    uncut and once killed ten times and resumed, recorded at one commit from a clean checkout. `recordings/README.md`
    says how each was recorded and what its counters show.
  - A test holds every bundled recording to the format, with the blobs it names, and its manifest's cost and processes
    to the counters' fold; the source cost check (`scripts/recording/fold.test.mjs`) covers the gallery too.
  - The README says what the deploy buttons publish: this replay viewer only, which never runs an agent or calls a model
    and asks for no key. A test parses both deploy configs and both buttons' URLs and fails on any variable, secret or
    binding.
  - A run of one process or one kill reads "1 process", "1 kill" in the gallery and the header.
- A second example, the hero job: one agent reads nine electricity grid operators' public dashboards in nine browser sessions at once, vision reads two numbers from each chart, and the run ends in `snapshot.html`, a table of the nine grids with each reading linked to the screenshot it came from and the operator's published figure beside it. `npm run demo:hero` runs it on fakes with no key; `-- --kill1` kills the run inside a paid call and resumes it without paying that call twice, and `-- --record <dir>` keeps the run as a recording. The nine dashboards were checked by hand (`examples/hero/sources.json`).
- **`apps/viewer`: a build that ships recordings as files**
  - `node apps/viewer/build.mjs --files` (or `build(target, dir, { files: true })`), off by default, writes
    `recordings/index.json` (each recording's id, manifest and blob paths) and a `gallery.js` that only points at it,
    instead of embedding every recording's lines and blobs in `gallery.js`. The page lists the gallery from the index and
    fetches a recording's lines when it is opened, its blobs at the paths the index names, with no guessing. It needs a
    static host; the default build still opens from `file://`.
- **`apps/viewer`: the manifest's job and store**
  - A manifest's `job` (the job that ran, a JSON blob) and `store` (the run's store at its end, a SQLite blob, with the
    commit and generation it was taken at), from the format's draft 24, are linked in the header: the job opens in a tab,
    the store downloads (`.sqlite` is never opened in the viewer's page).
  - Both count among the blobs a recording names, so a recording opened from a URL fetches them, and the viewer's format
    check notes either one missing from `blobs/`.
- **`apps/viewer`: the gallery is hosted on GitHub Pages**
  - `.github/workflows/pages.yml` builds the viewer and publishes it to https://parcha-ai.github.io/agentrun/ on each
    push to `main` that touches `apps/viewer/`, or by hand. Its token reads the repository and writes Pages; it names no
    secret.
  - It publishes nothing unless what was built passes `scripts/recording/gate.mjs`: every recording in it (a hidden one
    too) passes the recording validator; a recording holds only its manifest, its lines and blobs named by their own
    SHA-256; every published text file is free of credential shapes and machine paths; and the gallery embeds exactly the
    recordings beside it. An empty gallery fails.
  - `scripts/recording/gate.test.mjs` refuses each of those; `scripts/pages-workflow.test.mjs` holds the workflow's
    triggers, permissions and step order, and runs its gate step as written.
- **`apps/viewer`: a panel with nothing in a recording is not drawn**
  - A panel is drawn only for a recording that holds a line it is for: one its new optional `shows(line)` says yes to,
    or, without `shows`, one it `accepts`. With none, the shell draws nothing for it, not even its title. The shell
    judges this once over the whole recording, never up to the stop, so no panel appears or vanishes mid-scrub.
  - The transport panel declares `shows` for `transport` lines: it reduces the counters' lines for its own counters, so
    until now it drew an empty titled box on every run without transport lines.
- **`apps/viewer`: a scripted run says so**
  - The cost on the counters strip names a run whose manifest says `providers: "scripted"` (at least one priced part was a
    fake, a cassette or a script): "scripted, $0" rather than "$0.0000", or "scripted, $" and the price reported, because
    that is not a provider's bill, though it may include real charges for the other parts. A baseline's strip
    uses the baseline's own `providers`.
  - The header's meta line says "scripted providers" or "real providers" when the manifest says which.
- The viewer's panel contract can draw motion by time: a node's `vars` set CSS custom properties (numbers only), `ViewContext.now` is the playback clock, an `animated` panel is redrawn about ten times a second while playing, and a `featured(lines)` panel is shown first with the playback controls under it (the first registered one wins). `?t=<seconds>` opens the viewer at a time. Panels that set none of these draw exactly as before; see `apps/viewer/PANELS.md`.
- The viewer's transport panel is now a stage: one row per environment, the session token riding a rail between them, a flight shown as its own phases (drain, seal, ship, take, resume) with the token flying while the bytes ship, a whole-box kill as a red flash with the token left as a ghost and popping back in the rebuilt box, a process kill as a blink in place, a skipped wait as a hatched band with its seconds, the browser tile beside the lane it runs in, and the film's card at the end. The token, the lanes' marks and the status line all read one answer for where the session is. It is a pure view over the same reduced state (plus the box's lane and the lane the browser runs in), in light and dark and at phone width, and with `prefers-reduced-motion` it does not animate and tells the same states in words. It uses the new `featured`, `animated`, `vars` and `now` shell additions.
- The reference example's reconcile now hands a host's `rerun` the args the journal kept for an effect (`RecoveryEffect.args`). Before, a call admitted but never sent (a kill between admission and the send) could not be sent again after the kill, and the resumed run was refused with `FROZEN_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` even for a host that could rerun it. With a `rerun`, the resumed process now sends it exactly once.
- The viewer's transport panel no longer reads a host kill line with no `kind` as a whole-box kill: a kill's kind is `process` or `box`, an absent kind means a process, and only `box` is a box (the strip counts follow). A process that comes back with no box named no longer prints "at box ?". A recording with no transport lines now draws nothing from this panel, so a reference run's plain process kills no longer open an empty "Boxes" heading and a kills strip.
- `pi-1-2` fails unless every package with a pi-family peer is in its leg: each one is built, type-checked and tested on pi 1.2.0, so a package whose ranges stop accepting 1.2 fails CI instead of leaving the leg.
- The reference kill test no longer claims that the same seed lands every kill at the same place: under load, later landings depend on the order concurrent work takes inside a process. It asserts what is true: the same seed draws the same first kill's plan, another seed draws another, and every ledger check and gate passes on each run.
- `scripts/changes.mjs --check`, which the required `verify` job runs on pull requests and in the merge queue, fails a release commit (a workspace package version differs from the merge base's) while `changes/` holds anything but `README.md`: `release commit carries unfolded fragments: <names>; refold on current main`. The tag now always goes on the release pull request's own merge commit, with no separate fold pull request.
- **The examples' Since cells are enforced by the stranger test**
  - `scripts/stranger.mjs` reads the examples index (`packages/pi-durable-disk/examples/README.md`) and, for every keyless
    example whose Since is at or below the registry's `beta` tag, installs exactly that published version in an empty
    directory (the index's own install line), copies the example's files, runs `node main.ts` and expects its `ok:` line. An
    example above the beta is skipped with the reason printed. It runs in the existing `stranger` job through the
    `pi-durable-disk` spec (`examples` key), so it needs no workflow change; `node scripts/stranger.mjs pi-durable-disk
    --examples` runs only this check. An example that claims a Since its imports do not have fails, naming the missing export.
- The validator sweep collector (`spec/lean/sweep/collect.mjs`) takes `--test-concurrency <n>`, or `TEST_CONCURRENCY=<n>`, and passes it to the DSL, Pi and example suites it runs. With neither set, node's default is unchanged.
- The release's last step, `verify-published`, installs its consumer with the same `@earendil-works` pins the package check uses, and fails if the install lands on a version the repository lockfile does not hold. Before, the consumer floated pi-codemode to a release that dropped an export pi-coding-agent imports, and the step failed after every package had published.

  The floor legs of `scripts/pi-leg.mjs` pin the packages the family depends on outside it (pi-codemode and pi-mcp under the coding agent) at the leg's version too, and fail a leg whose coding agent loads one at another version. Before, they stayed green only because the lockfile held pi-codemode 1.1.0; a refresh to 1.2.0, which dropped an export that coding agents 1.0.4 and 1.1.x import, would have broken them.
- The install line and the requirement in the package README name Pi 1.2.0, the tested host. `npm install -g @earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@1.1.0` now installs a Pi that crashes on start: its `^` range pulls `pi-codemode` 1.2.0, which no longer exports `MCP_TYPESCRIPT_PREAMBLE`.
- **`apps/viewer/RECORDING.md` follows the format's draft 25**
  - A supervisor's `kill` line may say what was killed: `kind` is `process` when only the run's process died and `box`
    when its whole machine or sandbox was taken away. Absent means `process`, and a panel never guesses `box`.
  - A JSON result blob may be `agentrun.cited/1`: rows of a label, a value and its cites, each naming a blob, a look or
    both, with a fragment in pi-vision's form, and optionally the `url` the evidence was read from and the time `at`. A
    cite with a url and no blob or look is shown as linked, not kept; a row with no cite says on screen that it cites
    nothing.
  - The manifest may name the `job` that ran and the run's `store` at the end, each as a blob, and other recorded runs
    of the same demo in `see_also`, linked only.
  - Repeated and asked twice count the browser's replay-unsafe page actions, by `(conv, tool, codeSha)` from the session
    records' `pendingEffect` entries: each later dispatch of an action that already reached the page counts once, and one
    after a dispatch that shows no sign of reaching it is possible, never proven. A recording that keeps only the latest
    session records shows page actions not measured.
  - A look live lines show started is not a possible extra payment when a later process filed its key as kept: that
    filing is the same answer read back, already counted.
- A runnable example of one agent behind a tiny web page (`examples/web`): a job history, a live run page with chat, grouped steps, files and the agent's browser, and a hand-over where the agent asks the person to sign in through the browser's live view (`request_sign_in` ends its turn until the person replies). It runs on the open packages: the agent lives in an agent home of `@parcha/pi-durable-agent`, each run is a `pi-durable-disk` run directory started with `startRun` and served by its own door, the steps are `@parcha/pi-durable-agent/timeline` rows, and the server releases a run once it has answered and carries its Browserbase sign-in back to the home, so the next run starts signed in. `AGENT_BROWSER=chrome` runs the agent on its own headless Chrome instead, with its profile in the agent home, and mounts pi-browser's live view for it where that pi-browser has one; Browserbase stays the default until pi-browser ships `./live-view`. Its tests run offline.

## 0.1.0-beta.13 (2026-10-14)

- `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable` reads and writes tool results the same way on pi-durable 1.0.4 to 1.2.x, in one build; pi-durable 1.2 names a tool result's blocks `output` instead of `content` and reads a result that has only `content` as empty, with no error. The peer ranges do not move yet.
  - Every tool result the package writes carries its blocks under both keys: the record tool's answers and its fatal return, and `reuseReceipts`' refusal and its answer from a receipt. `toolResult(blocks, fields)` builds such a result for a host's own tools.
  - **Behaviour change:** a call through `reuseReceipts(...).wrap` whose result has its blocks only under the key the loaded pi-durable does not read now throws `ToolResultKeyError` (code `TOOL_RESULT_KEY`, with the tool's name), and no receipt is written. Before, on 1.2, the model received an empty result and the receipt stored an empty text. Results are read under the loaded version's key, so a failure envelope in a 1.2 result is no longer recorded as paid.
  - A Session that fails at a storage error (pi-durable 1.2 then rejects later calls with `SessionFailed`) reaches the recovery driver as the storage's own message and code, with the `SessionFailed` as its cause, and a run's `wait()` rejects with the storage's error, as on 1.1.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/recovery`: the host's effect adapter is told the admitted effect. Under the driver, `runEffect` receives `effect: { id, argsHash }` (typed on `RecoveryEffectParams`, beside `call`): the effect's journal id and the hash of its arguments, as `journal.admit` stored them and the same in every process of the run. A host that keeps what an outside call answered under them settles an effect whose call was cut with `journal.complete`, without recomputing the driver's hash. Additive; the journal's stored format is unchanged.
- CI gains two jobs. `pi-1-2` builds, type-checks and tests every package whose pi-family peer ranges accept pi 1.2.0 on that version, while the workspace develops on 1.1.0: `scripts/pi-leg.mjs` picks the packages from their ranges, installs one copy of each family package at the version, and fails on any other resolved copy. `agentrun-pi-pi-floor` runs `@parcha/agentrun-pi` with all six pi-family packages at 1.0.4, beside `@parcha/pi-durable-disk` at 1.0.4.
- New example, `examples/reference/` (`npm run demo:reference`): one program that composes the open packages on pi-durable and runs one job end to end with no key and no network but loopback. A disk run on a local directory, the workflow as one pi-durable task (`createWorkflowExtension`) with its LLM step on the node runner, the browser on its fake backend, vision's `read` and a scripted judge. `host.ts` declares what a host supplies as one value, `assemble.ts` wires it and `run()` returns the task's outcome (`complete`, `escalated`, `refused` with its code and message, or `stopped`). The tool door keeps its answers, so a run killed between a call's dispatch and its commit is settled from the answer before the driver opens; each judge's receipt is kept in the run's store and replayed by a resumed run; the gates read the run's store, not its status rows; and the scripted lead asks an errored read again, looks before asking a cut page action again and decides from the browser's details, and never cites a read of a page it did not open, `--json` prints the result as one object, `--dir` keeps the run so a second process resumes it, and `--chrome` drives a local Chrome that reaches only the job's pages. `examples/reference.test.mjs` pins the output and kills a run after the browser read, then resumes it. Its README lists the package gaps it works around.
- New package, `@parcha/pi-transport`: run a pi-durable session's tools in another machine. This release is its first layer: the `Box` and `BoxProvider` interfaces (create, attach, list, exec with streamed output and input, upload, download, status, destroy, an optional snapshot with a declared kind, coded `BoxError`s returned as values), `localBoxes` (`./box/local`) and `dockerBoxes` (`./box/docker`), the strict `transport` config (`parseTransportConfig`: unknown keys fail with their path, every reference names a declared environment), and a box conformance suite in `./testing` (`registerBoxConformance`, 19 cases).
  - Every box has a required maximum lifetime that its provider enforces with no call from the host, a program in a box never receives the host process's environment, and a provider refuses a spec field it cannot honour. The first two are conformance cases; the lifetime case observes the machine, not the provider.
  - It does not depend on the other packages. Publishing it is on hold: its manifest is `"private": true`, so it is built, tested, verified and kept at the workspace version but is in no release (see `docs/releasing.md`). Its README and `packages/pi-transport/CHANGELOG.md` have the details.
  - It depends on `typebox`; pi-durable and chord (1.0.4 to 1.1.x) are peers. It follows pi's own code style and tooling inside the package: Biome, and Vitest on source through a `source` export condition. Both are development dependencies of this package only.
  - CI gains two jobs: `pi-transport-pi-floor` runs its build, type-check and suite again on pi-durable 1.0.4, and `transport-docker` runs the box conformance suite on real containers.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable` stores a run's decision rows in pi-durable: the session document `agentrun.decisions` (document version 1, row version 1), `appendDecision`, `readDecisions`, and `createDecisionWriter({ open })`, which commits rows in the order they are recorded, opens the run's Session on the first row, reports the first failed commit from `settled()` and `flush()`, and releases the Session in `flush()` even after a failure. The entry point now imports pi-durable and chord at runtime; both stay optional peers of the package, needed only by a host that imports `./durable`.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable` runs a workflow's LLM nodes on pi-durable. `nodeRunner(host)` returns one extension to install before the Harness opens, and `on(scope)` gives the `runNode` that `withRecovery(driver, { runNode }, { durableNodes: true })` calls once per attempt and the `closeStepSession` that `openRecovery` takes. A host passes `agent()` and, as it needs them, `stance`, `record`, `verify`, `onNodeOpen`, `init`, `submit`, `digest` and `maxAttempts`. One attempt is one conversation, found again after a crash: an attempt that already delivered makes no request, a turn that committed is never requested again, and nothing of a resumed node reaches a model before the host's `onNodeOpen` ran. `legacy(entry)` gives a conversation an older build opened its extension back under that build's name.
  - The record core the node's `submit` tool is built from is exported with it. `deliver(contract, store, args)` decides one call over a small store interface: transport repair, the file envelope under a host-named key, the delivery attempts, one feedback round of a second reading, and the model-facing texts. `lintRecordSchema` reports what an author should fix in a record schema, for a host or a validator: a root that admits no object, and a schema that declares its fields and requires one outside them. `recordTool`, `recordNudge` and `workspaceRecordFile` put it on a pi-durable conversation, and `runNodeAttempt(scope, attempt)` is the kernel under the runner (`taskScope`, `hostScope`).
  - Four document kinds. `agentrun.record` (`{ record, attempts }`) and `agentrun.gate` (`{ bounced, disagreements }`) are conversation documents, and `agentrun.nodes` is a session document family keyed by the attempt's session id (`conversation`, `digest`, `startedMs`, `closed`). `agentrun.node` is new: a conversation document with the node's system text, label, schema and file key. It is additive, and a conversation an older build opened gets one when the runner first reaches it.
  - A node that delivers no record throws `NodeFailure` (`code: "NODE_NOT_DELIVERED"`). `final: true` says a second attempt would spend the same again: the delivery attempts are spent, the model did not answer past pi's retries (pi's reason and detail are on `unanswered`), or the schema is one no `submit` call can satisfy (`lintRecordRoot`: it is no JSON Schema object, or its root type admits no object), which is refused before any conversation exists. A schema that requires a field outside its `properties` is not refused: the tool's parameters are open and the node's task carries the schema, so a model can deliver on it. `final: false` says the node's run ended with no record and attempts left, so a driver may admit a second attempt, a new conversation. An attempt found under another binding digest is refused with `NodeBindingMismatch` (`code: "NODE_BINDING_MISMATCH"`). A pause rethrows the run's stop and leaves the conversation for the attempt's next entry; any other stop aborts the conversation's work. A host whose `onNodeOpen` throws fails the node's call with that error and releases nothing: what pi resumed of the node waits for the attempt's next entry, or ends with the Harness's close.
  - Two limits of the attempt count, each pinned by a test. The nudge that spends the last attempt still continues the run, so a run can take one request more than its attempts and the stored count can read one past the limit. A rejection cut by a crash after its commit is decided and counted again when the call replays, so a crash there costs the node one attempt.
  - `spec/lean/AgentRunEnvelope/Gate.lean` models the gate's delivery (attempts, the one feedback round, termination), with an audit that fails when its list of theorems changes; `lake build` builds it by default.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable` runs a workflow as one pi-durable task. `createWorkflowExtension(host)` returns the extension to install before the Harness opens and the host's three operations on a run: `start(harness, { key, workflow, input, bind? }, context)`, `read(harness, key, context)` and `stop(harness, key, "pause" | "cancel", context, source?)`. The task is `agentrun.workflow`, version 1, with one phase: it opens the recovery driver over `documentStore` as the journal's one owner, runs the interpreter to its end inside one invocation and makes one terminal commit. A process that dies leaves the task pending, and the next process's `harness.resume()` runs the phase again over the same journal, which answers what committed and refuses an effect left unknown. The host gives the driver's options (`options`), then either its adapters, which the task wraps with `withRecovery`, or the dependencies it built over the open driver, with its own `interpret` when it runs its documents under its own policy (`run`).
  - A run's key names one task. The session document family `agentrun.task` (version 1, keyed by the run's key, `{ task, stop }`) is new and additive, and the journal's documents are unchanged. The entry and its task are created in one commit, so two starts of one key make one task. `start` reports `created`, `attached` (the task was live) or `settled`: a task that ended complete, escalated or cancelled is returned as it is, and one that ended refused or failed is replaced by a new task over the same journal, which refuses again until the host reconciles it. A journal an older build started, with no entry, gets its task at the next start.
  - A start that reaches a task asks for the run that task holds. Its workflow, what the host binds and its input are compared with the task's stored request by canonical digest, so key order does not matter. A request for another run is refused with the driver's `RUN_STORE_BINDING_MISMATCH`, whose `moved` names the workflow's parts, what the host binds, or the input's fields (`input.<field>`), and the start commits nothing: no task, no stop, no cancel. Before, a changed request reused its key silently: a live task ran its original request, a finished one returned its old outcome, and a replaced one ran the changed input over the earlier run's journal when that run had failed before its first step. The driver's binding is unchanged; it does not cover the input.
  - `wait` gives a `WorkflowOutcome`: `complete`, `escalated`, `refused` or `stopped`. A typed failure is a committed outcome, never a faulted task: `refused` carries its code and message, and `detail` the failure's own plain fields. A run whose terminal commit the Session refuses fails its waiters in that process with the run's own failure instead of leaving them waiting.
  - A pause reaches a run where it is live, in this process. `stop(..., "pause")` resolves `paused` once the run's work has stopped with the pause in its journal (an effect in flight completes and commits first, see the next entry), `ended` when the run ended otherwise, and `not-live` when it is not running here. The task stays pending; the host closes the Harness, and the next open continues the run with no second dispatch. A cancel is pi's `abortTask` and resolves once the task is marked: pi ends the run's invocation and the conversations the task owns, then the task's abort handler commits `cancelled` in the journal and an `aborted` outcome. The effect a cancel cut stays unknown, and a cancelled run never resumes. The first cancel names the run's source, and a later one does not change it. A stop given with the start (`options.stop`) is recorded in the commit that creates or reaches the task and is taken before any step; a cancel that a process recorded and died before pi marked the task is taken by the run's next invocation, before any step and before the host's ladder. A pause given with a start that attaches to a run already running in this process is recorded and taken when the run's next invocation opens; `stop` pauses a run that is running here.
  - A host's ladder runs inside the task. With `escalated(ending, invocation)`, a run that did not complete and was not refused continues there in the same invocation, so the continuation the host opens is owned by the task and a cancel aborts it before the task. The host commits the escalation row through `invocation.driver.escalate` before anything acts on it; a run reopened after that commit goes back to the ladder with the row, and the interpreter does not run again. A refusal, a stop or an abort never ladders.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable`: three seams for a durable host.
  - Breaking: `readJournal(file, key)` and `durableDoc(file, kind, key?)` take the run's store file instead of a directory. They no longer look for `<directory>/durable/run.sqlite`, so a store is read where its host keeps it (pi-durable-disk keeps it at `store/run.sqlite` under the claim); a directory passed as the file throws. `committedRoutes(state)` is unchanged.
  - `decisionRecovery(invocation)` returns `{ recordDecision, decision }` for a workflow task, from the invocation the host's `run` is handed: `recordDecision` commits each decision receipt as a row through the task's own commit, in the order receipts are recorded, and `decision(id)` answers `recovery.decision` from the last answered row that run recorded under that id, so a resumed run does not ask the judge again. `DecisionRow` gains four optional fields, `decision_id`, `request`, `state_sha256` and `run_key`, which make the row the whole receipt again for the run that asked it; another run in the same Session is never answered from it. The row version stays 1; a row without them is read as before and never answers.
  - `WorkflowTaskHost.options` is documented as where a host settles an effect a killed process left unknown: an effect it completes there (`journal.complete`, before the driver opens) is answered from that result and is not dispatched again. No change in behaviour.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/recovery`: a pause lets an effect in flight complete. Breaking for a host that relied on a pause cutting an effect. `driver.stop({ action: "pause" })` refuses new work at once and is taken when no effect is in flight: an effect already admitted dispatches, completes and commits its receipt first, so a paused run resumes without a reconciliation. Before, a pause aborted the effect through the driver's signal and left it unknown. New work (a node's resume, an effect, a wait, a step's attempt) waits at the new `driver.seam()` until the pause is taken and is then refused with `FROZEN_PAUSED`; finished work still commits. A cancel is unchanged: it is immediate, and the effect it cuts stays unknown. The recovery models do not move: neither has a stop, a pause adds no state between an admission and its completion, and the state a cancel leaves is the one a crash leaves.
- New package, `@parcha/pi-vision`: eyes for pi agents. `read` keeps its name and its arguments and reads images fitted or tiled to the model's limits, PDFs, office documents, spreadsheets and video; what cannot be done is a typed miss, and every look a second model answers is filed with a receipt. It has a host-neutral core, the pi-durable adapter (`./durable`), the Node environment (`./node`), eyes on pi-ai (`./eyes/pi-ai`), the providers (`./providers/<name>`) and `./testing` (fake eyes, fixture builders, the provider conformance suite, the leak check). `look` is declared and not built: it throws `NotBuiltError`.
  - It does not depend on the other packages, and a release publishes it with them under the `beta` tag. Its README shows a first read that needs no key and nothing optional installed; its stranger spec (`scripts/stranger/pi-vision.json`) runs that read from the packed tarball in an empty directory with an empty environment, and fails when its output differs from the README's by a byte. Its README and `packages/pi-vision/CHANGELOG.md` have the details.
  - It depends on `unpdf`, `@hyzyla/pdfium`, `fflate` and `@xmldom/xmldom`; pi-durable and chord (1.0.4 to 1.2.x), pi-ai (`*`, the host's copy, as Pi's package guidance asks of a package that imports it) and Photon are optional peers. sharp is passed in by a host and is neither a dependency nor a peer, so its LGPL libvips binaries stay out of `docs/dependencies.md`.
  - CI gains two jobs: `vision-binaries` installs ffmpeg and poppler and runs the package's `test/binaries` suites, and `pi-vision-pi-floor` runs its build, type tests and suite again through `scripts/pi-leg.mjs` with pi-durable, chord and pi-ai at 1.0.4, one copy of each, the lowest its peers accept. The package develops against 1.1.0, and `pi-1-2` runs it on pi 1.2.0.
- `docs/dependencies.md` counts the copies a package resolves inside its own workspace directory. `scripts/export-source.mjs` recorded only root-level lockfile locations, so a package whose dependencies or peers resolve to workspace-local copies dropped out of their rows: 7 package/version pairs were missing (248 listed, 255 now), all permissive.
- `@parcha/pi-durable-disk` installs beside pi-durable 1.1: its peer ranges for `@earendil-works/pi-durable` and `@earendil-works/chord` widen from `~1.0.4` to `>=1.0.4 <1.2.0`, and it is developed against 1.1.0 with its whole suite run again on 1.0.4 in CI. Its store passes pi-durable 1.1.0's storage conformance suite, which now requires scans in either order; the Docker runtime image installs pi 1.1.0. See `packages/pi-durable-disk/CHANGELOG.md`.
- `@parcha/pi-durable-disk` installs beside pi-durable 1.1: its peer ranges for `@earendil-works/pi-durable` and `@earendil-works/chord` widen from `~1.0.4` to `>=1.0.4 <1.2.0`, and it is built and tested against 1.1.0. Its store passes pi-durable 1.1.0's storage conformance suite, which now requires scans in either order; the Docker runtime image installs pi 1.1.0. See `packages/pi-durable-disk/CHANGELOG.md`.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi` also takes `@earendil-works/pi-durable` and `@earendil-works/chord` as optional peers at `>=1.0.4 <1.2.0`; install lines select `@beta`.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi` targets Pi 1.1.0. Breaking for standalone use: `@earendil-works/pi-agent-core`, `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent`, `@earendil-works/pi-tui` and `typebox` move from exact dependencies to optional peer dependencies at `*`, which is Pi's own packaging rule (a second physical copy can shadow the host's). `pi install` needs nothing more; an application or the `agentrun-pi` CLI installs the peers itself. A host tool called from a workflow now receives Pi 1.1's tool context (`tools`, and an `executeTool` that answers with an error outcome, since a workflow node has no sibling tools). `PiRunError` moves to its own module so the workflow service loads without a Pi package installed. `/agentrun status` reports Pi 1.1.0 as the tested host.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl` records typed-question decisions. A host that supplies `recordDecision(receipt, exchange)` gets one `DecisionReceipt` per request (`judge`, `pick`, `sift`, `route`, an `ask` predicate, a `verify` review), awaited before the answers reach state, an event or a branch; a rejection fails the node with `WorkflowDecisionRecordError`. A receipt holds the questions, the answers, the rule with its thresholds and the action, and `rederiveDecision(receipt)` recomputes the action without a model call. See "Decision receipts" in `docs/host-integration.md`.
  - The decision events carry the receipt's id: `decision_id` on `judge.answered`, `route.chosen`, `ask.evaluated` and `verify.answered`, and `decision_ids` (one per request) on a sift's `judge.answered`. `runJudge` receives it as `decisionId`. The id is the request's identity, so the same request asked again has the same id.
  - A sift split over the host's limits records one receipt per request. When one request fails, the requests in flight are recorded as `cancelled`, and a split sift now refuses a request's answers as soon as they arrive instead of after every request has returned.
  - `recovery.decision(id)` answers a request from the receipt an earlier run recorded. Before, a resumed run asked again: a loop's `until: ask` could end the loop on an earlier pass's state after later passes had run, and a route could take another branch than the one already started. With the hook, both follow the recorded decision.
  - No change for a host that supplies neither hook, except the new event fields.
- The Lean model's `sift` no longer fails when its items exceed the question limit: like the interpreter, it refuses only a question set that cannot fit one request. The conformance corpus gains its first sift cases, a sift is scripted per item, and a case may set `limits.maxQuestionsPerRequest`.
- Formal models of recovery. `spec/receipts/Receipts.tla` (TLA+) and `spec/lean/AgentRunRecovery/Frozen.lean` (Lean) state what a recovery driver does with effects over a `RecoveryJournal` across crashes and failed commits: admitted before dispatch, one dispatch per effect, a completed effect answered and an unknown one refused on resume, nothing admitted after a failed commit. TLC checks seven properties on two configs, nine guard mutants must each be caught by its named property, and Lean proves the driver's properties for every number of steps and crashes. A new `receipts-spec` CI job runs the TLA+ checks when a change touches the spec, and a nightly workflow runs them on `main`. AGENTS.md and CONTRIBUTING.md add the rule: a change to modeled behavior changes the model in the same pull request.
- `applyHostOutputTypes(candidate, host, { normalizeType })` takes an optional normaliser: an authored artifact type (another case, dashes for underscores, an alias) is mapped to the host's name for it before the host's prose types are matched; a type it maps to null or undefined is left as authored, an exact name included. Without it a type is matched exactly, as before. `candidatePolicyErrors` still compares a candidate's artifact types to the host's list exactly.
- `@parcha/pi-durable-disk` revokes, with a fenced or deleted run's delegations, the ones its holders keep on their own private directories (`.archil/client-<id>`, listed with no path), when those holders hold nothing else. They block no run; before, every killed client left them orphaned, and they piled up and resurfaced in listings. Its CHANGELOG has the details.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl` admits a tool effect with the external call it makes. `RecoveryEffect` gains an optional `intent: { tool, argsHash }` and `RecoveryJournal.admit` an optional last `intent` argument; the driver admits a `via: "tool"` node with `gatewayIntentOf(node.tool, input)` (for a node that calls the gateway through the `fetch` wrapper, the tool the wrapper names and the hash of its inner arguments), and the intent is kept from admission, through completion, at every later open. Shell and executor effects get none, and an effect admitted without one has no `intent` key. Additive: a journal written before this change reads as before, and its unknown effects stay matched by name and hash alone. Before, a continuation could match a completed effect by its receipt but never an unknown one, so a tail dispatched an identical paid call again; `inheritableUnknowns` now carries the intent and `reuseReceipts` refuses a call whose key matches it. A store built for the store contract must keep the intent: the conformance suite gains a case for it, and a mutant store that drops it.
- `@parcha/agentrun-dsl/recovery` builds a handoff from an escalation the driver committed: `buildHandoff(journal, options)` returns the bounded, redacted block and digest a continuation reads first, the attempt written as files under `evidence/frozen/`, and the receipts and unknown effects the journal hands over (`inheritableReceipts`, `inheritableUnknowns`, `gatewayIntentOf`). An input field named like a credential is never copied, and a failed file write is reported on the handoff, not hidden.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable` keeps a run's paid receipts as a document: `ReceiptIndex` (the session document `agentrun.receipts`, document version 1, one entry per paid call keyed by tool and argument hash, with the paid result and the session that paid), `reuseReceipts({ tools, sessionId, answer, unknown })` and `seedReceipts(host, receipts, from)`. `reuseReceipts(...).wrap(tool)` records a paid call's result, answers a repeat of another session's call from the receipt and says so (when `answer` is set), and refuses a call equal to an effect whose outcome is unknown; a tool result that is an error, or whose text is a failure envelope, is not a paid result and is not recorded. `seedReceipts` puts a handoff's receipts in the document before a continuation's first request and keeps a receipt already held. A receipt holds the result's text (the text blocks, joined by a newline): another kind of block, an image for one, is not kept, and a repeat is answered with the text alone; a call that returned nothing is a receipt whose result reads `null`, and its repeat is answered, not dispatched. The document's kind, version and key are a stored format.
- `@parcha/pi-browser` is published from the next release, under the `beta` tag, with the other packages: its manifest no longer says `"private": true`. Its README shows one command that needs no Chrome and no key; its stranger spec (`scripts/stranger/pi-browser.json`) runs that command from the packed tarball in an empty directory with an empty environment, and fails when its output differs from the README's by a byte. It runs on pi-durable 1.1 and 1.2 (`@earendil-works/pi-durable` and `chord` `>=1.1.0 <1.3.0`; `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` stays `*`). Its README and `packages/pi-browser/CHANGELOG.md` have the details.
- `@parcha/agentrun-pi` accepts pi-durable and chord 1.2: both peer ranges are now `>=1.0.4 <1.3.0` (they ended below 1.2). Pi's own packages (pi-ai, pi-agent-core, pi-coding-agent, pi-tui, typebox) stay `*`, as Pi's package guidance asks. The workspace still develops on 1.1.0.
  - CI gains `pi-cross-version`: the package's crash tests, with every first process of a run in an install of the packed packages on pi 1.1.0 and every later process in one on 1.2.0. A run left by a 1.1 process (killed with a tool call in flight, mid-submit, at any crossing of a sweep, or paused) completes on 1.2 with no effect dispatched twice. `scripts/pi-ranges.test.mjs` holds every package's pi-durable and chord ranges below 1.3, and Pi's own packages at `*`.
  - **A documented limit.** A run that pi 1.2 left with a tool call in flight cannot be continued by pi 1.1: pi 1.2 keeps that call as a version 2 `pi.tool` task, and 1.1 waits on it. The only thing 1.1 writes is that task's mark from running back to pending, as pi does for any task a dead process left running. Continue such a run on 1.2, and do not downgrade pi under a live run. A test in `pi-cross-version` pins what holds: after the 1.1 process ends, every other row of the store is as it was, and 1.2 then completes the run with no request made twice.
  - New in `@parcha/agentrun-pi/durable`: `refuseNewerTasks(harness, context)` and `NewerTaskError`. A host that may open a store on pi-durable before 1.2 calls `await refuseNewerTasks(harness, context)` after `Harness.open` and before `harness.resume()`.
